| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/RecipePortionMenu.swift` | The SwiftUI half of F4b's per-food unit menu: `RecipePortionMenu` (a `Menu` holding one sectioned inline `Picker` over `RecipePortionPicker.Choices`), `RecipePortionGramsRow` (the "USDA typical size, estimate" / "Your size" badge and the editable grams for one), `RecipePortionAskGramsRow` (fix round 1, report §6.3 Rung E: "How many grams is one?" where nothing on the menu counts or a line's unit does not convert), `RecipeTypicalSizeEstimateNote` (the estimate note under a saved line on the recipe page and in cooking mode), and `RecipeHouseholdCaption` ("Counted as 2 eggs (100.6 g)", inflected with `^[…](inflect: true)`). The menu's VoiceOver value is the current choice. |

## AI Context And Providers

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/AIContext/AIContextPayload.swift` | `AIContextPayload` marker protocol and concrete payload types (food-selection, meal-decomposition, etc.) defining the exact allowlisted fields that may enter each AI prompt. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/AIContext/MemoryAgent.swift` | Gate routing Tier-2 memory into AI prompts through a destination allowlist, recency filter, confidence filter, and diagnostic-language post-classifier, returning a char-capped context string. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/AIContext/AIAuditLog.swift` | `AIAuditEntry` metadata record plus the in-session `AIAuditLog` actor logging each AI call's payload kind, destination, included field names, and memory char count — metadata only, never persisted. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/AIProviders/FoundationWorkoutAdjustment.swift` | On-device workout adjuster: `WorkoutAdjustmentCandidateBuilder` builds the equipment/injury-filtered, ranked, capped candidate pool and `FoundationWorkoutAdjustmentModel` maps a natural-language request to substitutions within it via FoundationModels. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/AIContext/AICapabilityTier.swift` | The minimum capability an AI task declares, plus its ordered `escalationLadder`. The router picks the *cheapest available* destination meeting the tier, never above device capability or the user's ceiling (Ladder §3.1). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/AIContext/AIDeviceCapability.swift` | Snapshot of which AI rungs the *device* can physically reach, independent of user settings; obtained through the `AIDeviceCapabilityProviding` seam so tests can inject a fixed value. `.webNutritionLookup` always reports false by design (settings-gated web path, not an LLM rung). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/AIContext/AICallQuota.swift` | The daily AI-call budget with day-key rollover (Ladder §3.2). A pure value the *caller* stores device-locally — the derived `.sleepy`/`.resting` states must never reach synced `FernletSettings`, or one device's usage would throttle another. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/AIContext/FernletModelRouter.swift` | Capability-capped route resolution and its `AIRouteResolution`/fallback-reason vocabulary, letting deferred tasks tell a transient budget fallback from a persistent one before spending a retry. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/AIContext/FernletAIGate.swift` | The single routing entry point every AI call site funnels through — router + stored user intent + device-local quota in one value. `dispatch`/`resolveRoute` are the only places the daily quota is charged. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/AIProviders/SystemLanguageModelCapabilityProvider.swift` | Production `AIDeviceCapabilityProviding`: wraps `SystemLanguageModel.default.availability`. The PCC and BYOK rungs report false here — their symbols are iOS 27 APIs. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/AIProviders/FoundationIngredientSubstitution.swift` | On-device stage for F4 substitution: the model contributes world knowledge only (substitute food *names* plus an optional reason) — never a quantity, macro, or binding. Code rebinds each name through the local catalog. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/AIProviders/FoundationJournalSummary.swift` | On-device stage for the journal → Core Memory summary: `light` tier (never leaves the device), ambient (yields to the sleepy band), fed only the entry via `JournalSummaryPayload`; the reply must pass `JournalMemorySummaryPolicy` or it is audited as a fallback and dropped. No retry by design. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/JournalMemorySummarizer.swift` | `JournalMemorySummarizing`, the seam `FernletStore` asks for a journal memory's summary, and its production conformer over the walled stage; a test double stands in because the model never runs in the simulator. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/FernletAIComposition.swift` | Composition-root factory for the AI provider seam. Lives in its own file on purpose: naming `SystemLanguageModel…` matches the S3 grep-wall's AI-facing marker, keeping the type out of `FernletStore.swift`. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/AICallQuotaStore.swift` | The device-local, non-synced daily AI-call counter backed by `UserDefaults`. Deliberately app-target-only: the walled AI module reaches it solely through the `AICallQuotaStore` protocol declared in `AIContext`. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/FileAIAuditLogStore.swift` | Device-local, non-synced persistence sink for the AI audit log (Ladder §7.2) — one JSON file in Application Support, reached only through the `AIAuditLogPersisting` protocol. |

## Health And Launch Services

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/HealthKitGateway/HealthKitService.swift` | HealthKit service protocol and implementation, capability metadata, authorization snapshots, body/activity/cycle/mindfulness context, and authorization view model. Since 2026-09-23 also THE write gate: every write into Apple Health passes `requireWriteSharing(_:)` (master switch + that kind's switch) and reaches the store only through `save(_:)` or the `HealthKitStoreControlling.saveWorkout` seam, where the workout builder now lives. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/HealthKitGateway/WorkoutHealthKitSync.swift` | HealthKit workout import pipeline; reads `HKWorkout` samples and upserts matching `Workout` records via a `WorkoutSyncContext`. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/HealthSyncCoordinator.swift` | `HealthSyncCoordinator` (`HealthSyncContext`) — HealthKit ingestion (daily-context merge, derived sleep, workout import/backfill/observe) extracted from `FernletStore`. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/CoreDataHealthKitCacheCleaner.swift` | `CoreDataHealthKitCacheCleaner` — concrete `HealthKitCacheClearing` that scrubs HealthKit values an older build left in the synced rows / blob and empties the device-local HealthKit residue cache, fail-closed so opt-out never silently "succeeds"; its capture mode is the one-time launch scrub. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/DeviceHealthResidueStore.swift` | `FileDeviceHealthResidueStore` (the device-local, backup-excluded, bounded JSON cache of this device's HealthKit readings; one `production` instance) and `InMemoryDeviceHealthResidueStore` (the synchronous initializer's hermetic default). |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/FernletStore+HealthBodyProfile.swift` | `FernletStore` extension: this device's HealthKit body-profile import (age, sex, height, weight) recorded device-locally instead of into the synced `settings.userProfile` (2026-09-23), `effectiveUserProfile`/`effectiveSettings`, the profile-screen edit split (only changed fields reach settings), the opt-out reload, and the `DeviceHealthBodyProfile(imported:)` bridge from the gateway type. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/FernletStore+ProximityDebugTools.swift` | `FernletStore.proximityDebugToolsEnabled`: the Release clamp on the synced `showProximityDebugTools` setting (the stored value in DEBUG, always false in Release, where no switch exists to turn a synced `true` off). Gates the Friends tab's raw discovery diagnostic, the recipe-share "Connection details" log, the Force override and the Connection Inspector entry. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/FernletStore+RecipeShare.swift` | `FernletStore` extension (2026-09-30): `recipeShareDraft(for:)` (the one draft every Share-screen entry point builds), `recipeShareText(for:includesNotes:)` (through `RecipeShareText`, replacing the two old builders) and `recipeMessagesCardOffer(for:includesNotes:)` with its pure `messagesCardOffer` — `ready` with the packet only when the card's URL fits, else `tooLarge`, `webImported` (the pinned no-ingredients defect is not widened) or `unavailable` (`RecipeMessagesCardOffer`). |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/FernletStore+HealthKitResidue.swift` | `FernletStore` extension: records today's HealthKit residue at the ingestion points, and the once-per-device launch scrub of HealthKit values out of rows older builds synced. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/WorkoutHealthAccessOffer.swift` | `WorkoutHealthAccessOffer` + `WorkoutHealthAccessOutcome` — the one contextual Apple Health ask at the first workout logged or started (onboarding's "Asked the first time you log a workout…"): mirrors "Give access" (master + workout switches, prompt, ledger), restores the switches on a decline, and never asks twice, after an explicit off in Settings, or when HealthKit would show no sheet. Its durable fact `fernlet.healthkit.workoutAccessOfferResolved` lives in the store's sensitive-surface suite and is cleared by "delete everything". |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/HealthAccessGrant.swift` | `HealthAccessGrant.requestInContext` — asking for one kind of Health data where a feature first needs it, with the effect Settings' "Give access" has: the kind's Fernlet switch goes on, the prompt runs, the ask is ledgered, and a declined prompt puts the switch back. Never turns the master switch on. Exists because the gateway's write gate (2026-09-23) reads that switch. The period sheet and Cycle page no longer call it (owner question Q4); kept so the ask can be reinstated in one call. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/WorkoutTombstoneStore.swift` | `WorkoutTombstoneStore` — persisted capped FIFO ring of removed workout ids so a resurrected orphan HealthKit sample is deleted-and-skipped rather than re-imported. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateHealthStore/CyclePredictionEngine.swift` | Statistical cycle prediction engine using historical period data to forecast next period start, flow patterns, confidence level, and predicted flow days. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/LaunchPreparationService.swift` | Launch preparation state and photo wall seed loading. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletFoundation/StartupTiming.swift` | `os_signpost`-based startup timing probes for measuring and instrumenting app launch phases. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/AppServices/NotificationService.swift` | Thin `UNUserNotificationCenter` wrapper for the opt-in gentle daily check-in local reminder: authorization, status check, and scheduling. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/AppServices/WeatherKitService.swift` | Opt-in coarse-location WeatherKit service producing tiny derived snapshots (`WeatherComfort` walk-friendliness, `AmbientSky` sky bucket) for mood-recovery prompts and Home ambience; degrades to nil, leaking no raw data. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletFoundation/FernletDate.swift` | Layer-0 date helpers (`FernletDate`): canonical `yyyy-MM-dd` day-key formatting, enumeration, and display. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletFoundation/MonotonicClock.swift` | `MonotonicClock` protocol plus sleep-counting `SystemMonotonicClock` (`mach_continuous_time`) for the store-ban clock. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletFoundation/FernletAuditLog.swift` | Privacy audit-log facade (`FernletAuditLog`) emitting os_log events with a test-observable capture-handler registry. ProximityKit's lines arrive through FernletConnections' `FernletAuditBridge`, the sink `FernletApp.init` installs (plan step A0.2.10). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletFoundation/PersistenceFailureAudit.swift` | `PersistenceFailureAudit`: the one seam every ENVIRONMENTAL persistence failure records through — a Core Data fetch/save/delete, a file write/remove, or the payload encode that feeds one. Replaced the `assertionFailure`-in-`catch` those sites carried (a DEBUG trap on a locked-device write). Records the frozen dotted token plus the error's `NSError` domain and code only — never `localizedDescription` or `userInfo`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletFoundation/BackupExclusion.swift` | Layer-0 helper (`BackupExclusion`) toggling `isExcludedFromBackupKey` across a Core Data store file and its sidecars. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletFoundation/FernletFoundation.swift` | Overview/placeholder source standing up the layer-0 `FernletFoundation` module. |

## Social And Mesh Networking

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshNetworkManager.swift` | Observable mesh network coordinator managing active/lightweight peer slots, proximity-join (15 cm commit gate), **the founding at the first commit** (P6 item 2: the descriptor, the membership ledger, the founder admission, the ceiling and the state machine, with `unwindNewbornMesh()`/`yieldsNewbornMesh(_:to:from:)` repairing the double mint, `reassertLocallyClosedMode()` keeping this user's own closed choice against any descriptor — a sticky local policy plus a monotonic `modeSetAt`, because LWW on wall clocks lost it both ways — `resumeSearchingForPartitionedMesh()` re-arming a mesh that outlived its links, `endSessionAfterDiscoveryTimeout()`, and `isSessionLive` carrying the session-end lifecycle that neither `isInSession` nor `hasCommittedPeer` can: session end means MESH end, so a link blip runs no part of the ceremony), session open/closed state, member removal proposals, admission requests, friend-of-friend vouchers, photo session metadata, encrypted mesh payloads, coordinator beacons, and key rotation. Its observation loop explicitly finishes cancelled streams so repeated Friends-tab discovery sessions do not retain suspended observers. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/Proximity/Feasibility/NetworkMeshFeasibilityProbe.swift` | DEBUG-only Bonjour/QUIC probe with a Simulator infrastructure lane and a physical-device peer-to-peer/background gate; verifies the signed TLS-channel binding. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/Proximity/Feasibility/MeshRejectionMatrixHarness.swift` | DEBUG-only launch hooks for the runbook's Lane C (two Simulators, the PRODUCTION mesh over QUIC): `MeshMatrixDebugOptions` reads `FERNLET_MESH_MATRIX*` once per process, and the harness seeds a memory-only `MeshDescriptor` roster, echoes this device's signing identity, and starts the radios so each named `MeshIntroductionRejection` can be observed on a real radio. Off when absent; compiled out in release. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/Proximity/Feasibility/MeshFlowDriver.swift` | DEBUG-only Lane C app-flow driver: `FERNLET_MESH_FLOWS=<csv>` commits each device's own slot at either proximity gate and then drives chat, photos and the clothing shop through the same public entry points the UI uses, echoing both sides' observations under `[mesh-flow]`. Off when absent; compiled out in release. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/DisposableCameraView.swift` | Active Friends-session disposable-camera UI: `CameraCaptureController` (`@Observable`, queued `AVCaptureSession` lifecycle, rotation handling, and arm/wind gate), `CameraPreviewView`, and the full-screen camera. The session-info sheet exposes the 10-shot film count, open/closed access, mesh rename, participant options, friend-of-friend labels, diagnostics, removal requests, and end-session flow; admission requests and mesh errors are presented as popups. The Develop review offers the photo ids snapshotted at Develop and renders/answers them wherever the manager holds them (`photosAwaitingAnswer(among:)`, `finishSessionPhotos(keeping:of:)`), so door 3's give-up moving the roll under the open review cannot void the person's answer; it reports whether any sheet or alert of its own is up to `FriendsView` (`presentsOwnSheet`). Since 2026-09-30 the review's tiles load held photos through `reviewThumbnailData(for:)`, the answer is read before anything else happens (an unapplied one keeps the sheet up), and the camera-roll copy is the opt-in toggle, exported after the answer over `keptOnWall` from the wall. Since session photos U3 it reports its Develop review to `SessionPhotoReviewCoordinator.cameraDevelopReviewUp` from its presentation flag and clears it on the camera's AND the review sheet's disappear (the gate reads it ANDed with `isInSession`, I20), raises the coordinator's answer leg around its finish-and-leave (`beginAnswer()` / `defer { endAnswer() }`, I21), waits for a failed export's alert on the coordinator's `developSaveFailureAcknowledgement` (not a `@State` object: U3 fix round 1, so a termination or swipe-down never strands that leg), and stops its capture session while the overlay shows (`handleOverlayChange`), restarting only over a live session. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/JoinPromptSheet.swift` | `JoinPromptSheet` — the shared "someone wants to join" confirmation for both closed-group gatekeepers: mesh admission requests (presented by the disposable-camera session flow) and Group Activity join requests (presented by `ActivitiesView`). Generic over the request payload via a display-name extractor closure (callers pass the name through `PeerNameDisplay`; no fingerprint since 2026-09-29); swipe-to-dismiss declines everything still pending (fail-closed). Replaced the former `MeshAdmissionPromptSheet.swift` and `ActivityJoinPromptSheet.swift`. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/FriendListView.swift` | Friend list screen for browsing trusted proximity peers with all/friends/blocked filters and block management. Names render through `PeerNameDisplay` ("Someone you met" for a friend kept before their name arrived); the detail card's collapsed "Safety code" is the one place the app still shows a fingerprint. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/ActivitiesView.swift` | `ActivitiesView` — Group Activities screen (host form / nearby invites / hosting roster / joined states) off the Friends tab, driven by `ProximityActivityManager`. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/SessionChatPanel.swift` | `SessionChatPanel` — live-session chat panel over `MeshNetworkManager`: the memory-only transcript (cleared at session end) plus, since **P6 item 4**, an INLINE notice for a send that did not stage, shown at the TOP of the compose bar (above the text field, below the transcript — five docs said "beneath", corrected at item 4's fix review) with the draft kept. Reads `sendTempMessage(_:)`'s returned `MeshTextSendOutcome` rather than `routedShareRefusal`, whose one consumer is a `.alert` on the `DisposableCameraView` this panel is presented over. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/SafetyReportingView.swift` | `SafetyReportingView` — always-available report/block explainer and no-tolerance policy for shared shop items and people (App Store UGC compliance). |

## Proximity

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Engine/ProximityCoordinator.swift` | High-level coordinator for proximity sessions; orchestrates the per-peer transport channel its radio hands it (`PeerTransport`, never a framework type), the signed identity/ranging-token handshake, UWB startup, heartbeat RTT, payload dispatch, and inspector recording. Since ProximityKit plan step A0.3 it reads the host's vocabulary off its identity's namespace: it signs its introduction, acknowledgement and heartbeat with its reply under `family.vocabulary.session`'s tokens and titles and dispatches by them, parks a token outside `payloads.known`, and takes the wire2 token, the legacy assumption and its receive bound (`maxAdvertisedCapabilities(in:)`, twice the capability count) from `capabilities`; `PeerIdentity.supports(_:in:)` takes the host's capabilities, and its `ProximityCapability` form is the features' gate. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Engine/ObservationLoop.swift` | `ObservationLoop.start` — the shared `withObservationTracking` re-arm loop behind the proximity managers' coordinator-state observers (`MeshNetworkManager.startObserving`, `ProximityRecipeShareManager.startObserving`, `PresenceManager.startHeartObserving`), which each hand-rolled the same machinery until its leak-fix comment had drifted to only one copy. Holds the owner weakly and finishes the `AsyncStream` explicitly, so a cancelled discovery session leaves no suspended observer task behind. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Transport/PeerHandle.swift` | Transport-neutral peer value (`PeerHandle`) and the transport's stable endpoint identity (`PeerEndpointKey`), whose `isSameEndpoint(as:)` is the sanctioned "same device?" test. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Transport/PeerTransport.swift` | Framework-free transport protocol and its value family: `PeerTransport`, `PeerTransportState`, `PeerPendingInvite`, `InboundPeerFrame`, `PeerTransportError`, `PeerDeliveryMode`. Its discovery doors take no service type: the shared radio sessions own discovery on their namespace's service types. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Engine/ProximityCommitDetector.swift` | Rolling distance-window detector used by proximity commit and trainer tap gates. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Ranging/RangingProvider.swift` | Ranging provider contract plus shared distance and state models. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Transport/MeshTransportSelection.swift` | The friend mesh's radio seam: `MeshPeerChannel` (a per-peer channel that knows its peer — `NetworkPeerChannel` and `DetachedPeerChannel` conform), `DetachedPeerChannel` (the radio-less channel the manager's test seams use), and the `MeshTransportSession` conformance of `NetworkMeshSession` — **the only radio a shipping or a test build constructs since the deletion round (2026-09-22)**, which took `MeshTransportKind`, `MeshTransportFactory`, the DEBUG `FERNLET_MESH_TRANSPORT` bisect door and the MultipeerConnectivity conformance out with `MeshMultipeerSession.swift`. There is no selection left to make: `MeshNetworkManager.init`'s default is `NetworkMeshSession(namespace: namespace)` directly (built from the manager's stored namespace since ProximityKit plan step A0.2.7), pinned by `MeshTransportSelectionTests.theAppsInitializerRunsOnTheQUICRadio` and `MeshP9McRetirementAcceptanceTests`. The seam protocol stays because the suite injects a fake through it. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Transport/NetworkMeshSession.swift` | **The friend mesh's radio** since the cutover: the Network.framework/QUIC surface on `_fernlet-mesh2._udp` — listener, Bonjour browse, the signed channel introduction and its roster verdicts (including D-4.3's provisional stranger admission), the per-peer `NetworkPeerChannel` tunnels, the heartbeat, the re-propose sweep and the eviction-cause seam. Its decisions are factored out so tier 1 can enumerate them with no radio and no wall clock (`MeshLinkTable`, `MeshHeartbeatSchedule`, `MeshLinkAdvertisement`, `MeshSessionIdentityMap`, `NetworkMeshWire`, `MeshTransferStreamTable`, `EphemeralMeshTLSIdentity`); `Tests/FernletTests/NetworkMeshTransportTests.swift` is the battery and the runbook's device lanes cover the actor. The browse transcript logs its COUNT publicly and peer instance NAMES privately (plan §8.7 finding 1, taken at the cutover). Since ProximityKit plan step A0.2.7 it is built with `init(namespace:)` and reads its service type, ALPN, heartbeat, TLS exporter label (handed to `channelBindingHash(for:exporterLabel:)`), the labels every channel-introduction exchange frames and checks under, and its log subsystem off that namespace once, and the mesh instance-name prefix and TLS common name its instance names and certificates are minted under; the statics are gone. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Transport/NetworkPresenceSession.swift` | **P9 item 2 pass 2** (plan §17.1): the presence radio's Network.framework/QUIC surface on `_fernlet-near2._udp` — one listener registered under the current `PresenceEpochPosture`'s instance name and presenting its TLS identity, one browser, and up to four short-lived pairwise tunnels multiplexed into `NetworkPeerChannel`s. A separate, much smaller session than `NetworkMeshSession` on purpose (the posture rotates here and must never rotate there; there is no signed channel introduction, because `ProximityCoordinator`'s SEALED introduction is the presence handshake; there is no roster, dial budget, heartbeat or transfer stream) while reusing its building blocks by symbol: `ProximityQUICParameters`, `NetworkMeshWire`, `MeshSessionIdentityMap`, `MeshLinkKey`, `NetworkPeerChannel`. Owns **no timer**, so presence keeps its one-timer rule. Since ProximityKit plan step A0.2.7 it is built with `init(namespace:)` and reads its service type, ALPN and log subsystem off that namespace once. An inbound dialer is resolved by the `PresenceDialHello` it writes first and refused unless the owner's `resolveDialer` answers with an already-browsed, tag-matched peer. Audits `presence.quic.advertised` / `.rotated` / `.sighted` / `.connected` / `.redundantTunnelClosed` / `.stopped`. Pinned by `PresenceOverQUICTests`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Transport/NetworkRecipeShareSession.swift` | **P9 item 3 pass 2** (plan §17.1): the recipe-share radio's Network.framework/QUIC surface on `_fernlet-recipe2._udp` (ALPN `fernlet-recipe-v1`, both read off the host's namespace by `init(namespace:)` since ProximityKit plan step A0.2.7, with its log subsystem and the mesh instance-name prefix and TLS common name its postures are minted under) — one listener registered under a `RecipeSharePosture` minted fresh at every `start()` and every resume (random instance name + `EphemeralMeshTLSIdentity` + `sid`, no rotation timer), one browser, and the ONE pairwise tunnel the hard 2-device cap allows, multiplexed into a `NetworkPeerChannel`. `RecipeShareRadioSession` is the seam `ProximityRecipeShareManager` drives it through. What it adds that the presence radio deliberately has none of is a **per-transfer-stream acceptor**: a recipe crosses as ONE sealed frame, and a recipe carrying a picture clears `MeshTransferStreamTable.bulkFloorBytes` and earns a stream of its own (ack byte, bounded budget, refused for any connection this radio never admitted) while a text recipe stays in order on the control stream. `pauseDiscovery()` stands the listener AND the browser down for as long as a pairing is held — the shipped "recipe sharing closes once two devices connect" — and `stop()` clears the pause flag, which is what makes `RecipeShareDiscoveryGate`'s three `.unchanged` rows true. An inbound dialer is resolved by the `RecipeShareDialHello` it writes first and refused unless it is not our own `sid`, the owner's `resolveDialer` names a browsed peer, and the owner's `shouldAcceptDialer` admits it. Every `report` is a START failure or a *listener* failure; a dial miss (including a peer whose identity aged out), a refused hello and a failed transfer are audited and nothing more, and a BROWSER failure is stood down and audited (`recipe.quic.browserFailed`) but reaches the owner's door only when nothing is held and nothing is mid-connect. A withdrawn Bonjour registration REPUBLISHES under a fresh posture (`recipe.quic.registrationWithdrawn`) rather than leaving the radio dark with `isListening` still true — P8 item 0's device finding (b). The `PeerHandle` it mints carries NO `displayHint`: the only tokens it holds for a peer are the random instance name and the endpoint key containing it, so the name a user reads comes from the TXT `name` or from the picker's existing placeholder. Audits `recipe.quic.advertised` / `.paused` / `.resumed` / `.sighted` / `.connected` / `.dialRefused` / `.helloRefused` / `.redundantTunnelClosed` / `.transferStream` / `.transferStreamRefused` / `.transferStreamFailed` / `.browserFailed` / `.registrationWithdrawn` / `.stopped`, every peer named by an opaque salted session label. Pinned by `RecipeShareOverQUICTests`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Transport/MeshTransferStreamTable.swift` | The per-transfer stream policy for the QUIC radio: `MeshTransferRoute` (control stream vs a stream of its own), the 64 KiB bulk floor, the per-direction concurrency budgets, and the one-byte transfer ack. Pure and clock-free, so the whole policy is settled at tier 1. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshSessionTypes.swift` | Mesh session slot, group-key, ranking-window sample, participant, and encryption-error support models extracted from the manager; `MeshFriendReviewBatch` (a memory projection of the pending corpus plus the candidates) and, since 2026-09-30, `SessionPhotoAnswer` / `SessionPhotoAnswerFailure` — what one session-photo answer actually did, per photo, which the hosts read before hiding the review or exporting (`keptOnWall` only). `PeerSlot.supports(_:in:)` (a token, or a feature's `ProximityCapability`) asks under the host's capabilities, whose legacy assumption covers a peer that listed none. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshKeyAgreementAdvertisement.swift` | P6 item 1 (plan §11.3 item 13(ii)): `SignedKeyAgreementAdvertisement` (a member's own signed key-agreement public key, `fernlet.mesh.key-agreement.v1`, self-signed and with `meshID` bound into the transcript), `MeshKeyAgreementAdvertisementSet` (bounded at the admission cap, grow-only, and **conflict-refusing** — deliberately NOT a `MeshMembershipRecordSet`, whose earliest-wins merge would silently pick one of two keys), `MeshKeyAdvertisementFoldOutcome` / `MeshKeyAdvertisementFoldResult` / `MeshKeyAdvertisementFold` (the one pure fold door: the idempotence pre-filter, then verification, then the three-way decision) the `@MainActor` `signed(...)` mint, `MeshKeyAdvertisementFold.restoring(_:verifiedBy:)` (the restore door: every persisted row re-proved against the ledger adoption proved, drops named), `MeshKeyAgreementPayload` (the additive unsigned wire frame, a batch clamped to the set's capacity on both doors) `MeshKeyAdvertisementReceiveBounds` (the per-sender frame bound the membership family otherwise has none of — `capacity × transitionsPerMember`, recomputed by the second fix review once the conflict-mark release added a third per-member transition), the park's own four types (`MeshKeyAdvertisementPark`, `MeshParkedKeyAdvertisement`, `MeshParkedKeyAdvertisementOffer`, `MeshKeyAdvertisementParkOutcome`) under `MeshKeyAdvertisementParkBounds` — the per-SENDER side container for rows refused `signerNotAdmitted`, which a flat member-keyed map let one misbehaving member fill with a single frame — and `MeshKeyAdvertisementSendBounds` (`selfMintAttemptsPerSession` — the bound on the sender's repair of its own row; unbounded, it tripled a full test run). `MeshKeyAgreementAdvertisementSet.clearingConflicts(outside:)` is the bounded relief for the conflict mark's blast radius: a mark is dropped once the derived roster no longer names its member. **Addressing, not membership**: it verifies against `ledger.admissions` and can never create, end or revoke a membership, and it is not a fifth `MeshMembershipRecordKind` — so the membership digest's golden and the pinned re-gossip budget cannot move. Persisted inside `MeshSessionContext` at schema 3. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshRoutedManifest.swift` | P5 item 1 (plan §11): `MeshRoutedManifestFormat`, `MeshRecipientKeyWrap`, `MeshRoutedManifest` (the origin-signed routed-item record with its immutable destination set and per-recipient wraps, clamped on both doors), `MeshRoutedManifestPayload` (the `fernlet.mesh.routed-manifest.v1` frame), `MeshRoutedManifestMintError`, and the `@MainActor` `signed(...)` mint over a `MeshDeliveryTarget`, declaring against the routed type registry its caller passes (no default: the manager passes its own, built from its host namespace's routed types). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshRoutedManifestVerifier.swift` | P5 item 1: `MeshRoutedManifestVerifier` — the one receive-side door (mesh, shape, accepted type token, origin key from the ADMISSION ledger, origin not in the REMOVAL set, signature, wraps ≡ destinations, distinct set, expiry equality) — and its frozen `MeshRoutedManifestRejection` tokens. Public material only; departures never consulted (a departed origin verifies), quorum removals refused by name, no destination lookup. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshRoutedContentKeyWrapper.swift` | P5 item 1: `MeshRoutedContentKeyWrapper` — the per-recipient content-key wrap (ephemeral X25519 → HKDF → AES-GCM with the manifest binding and recipient in the AAD) and its inverse via a static-agreement closure, plus `MeshRoutedWrapBinding` and `MeshRoutedKeyWrapError`. Since ProximityKit plan step A0.2.6 `wrap`, `unwrap` and `additionalData` take `in purposes:`: the HKDF salt is the host namespace's `purposes.keyDerivation.meshRoutedContentKeyWrapV1` and the AAD label its `purposes.aead.meshRoutedContentKeyWrapV1`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshChunk.swift` | P5 item 2 (plan §11): `MeshChunkFormat` (256 KiB payload cap, the 1024-chunk cap derived from the 256 MiB content cap, the in-flight headroom constant, and `chunkCount(forSize:)`), `MeshRoutedContentDigest` (the item hash, the per-chunk hash and the derived chunk id, each under its own `Hash` purpose — since ProximityKit plan step A0.2.6 the caller's `in purposes:`, the host namespace's `purposes.hash`), `MeshChunk` (the origin-signed slice whose payload is EXCLUDED from the signed transcript and bound through `chunkHash`; its replay-window id is `chunkID(in:)`, a function since A0.2.6), and `MeshChunkPayload` (the `fernlet.mesh.routed-chunk.v1` frame, signed and unsealed). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshChunkVerifier.swift` | P5 item 2: `MeshChunkVerifier` — the receive-side door (mesh, shape, origin key from the ADMISSION ledger, origin not in the REMOVAL set, signature, chunk hash AFTER the signature, expiry equality, then the manifest cross-checks) — and its frozen `MeshChunkRejection` tokens. Public material only, so it verifies on a locked device; the manifest is OPTIONAL, because a chunk may arrive before it. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshChunker.swift` | P5 item 2: `MeshChunker` — the origin's mint (`chunk(of:at:for:identity:)` is the primitive so item 6 can stream at `blob + one chunk`; `chunks(of:for:identity:)` is the bounded run) and `MeshChunkMintError`. Refuses to mint for a manifest this device did not originate; the blob is opaque and no key is ever handled here. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshChunkAssembly.swift` | P5 item 2: `MeshChunkAssembly` — the order-independent reassembler with `MeshChunkRefusal`, `MeshChunkAdmission`, `MeshChunkBinding` and `MeshChunkCompletion`. Every input returns a verdict, a retransmission is a no-op, completion is impossible while unbound, and `.complete(blob:)` is the whole-item hash check — necessary but never sufficient for item 3's custody receipt, which needs durability first. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshChunkAdmissionRule.swift` | P5 item 3 (C13): `MeshChunkDescriptor`, `MeshChunkSetShape` and `MeshChunkAdmissionRule` — the per-chunk verdict and the manifest-binding verdict, extracted so the in-memory reassembler and the durable routed store reach each through ONE pure function. Item 2's assembly tests pass unmodified beside the extraction. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshContentMerge.swift` | P4 item 7 (plan §10.3): content merge — ordering and dedup on ingestion. `MeshContentKey`, `MeshMergeableContent`, `MeshContentOrder`, `MeshContentSet`, the three projections (`MeshMergedPhoto` / `MeshMergedMessage` / `MeshMergedHeart`) and `MeshContentLedger`. Pure values with a pure union, so reconnect, post-partition merge and reload-after-death are one code path; nothing here is persisted and nothing is wire. Since **P6 item 4's fix review** the union keys on `MeshContentKey` = `(author, id)` rather than the id alone, for every kind — a routed id is its origin's own choice and its signed manifest publishes it to the whole roster before the content arrives, so an id-only key let one admitted member spend another's dedup slot. §10.3's order is unchanged. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshRoutedItemSeal.swift` | P5 item 13: `MeshRoutedItemSealFormat`, `MeshRoutedItemSealError` and `MeshRoutedItemSealer` — the routed ITEM seal (AES-256-GCM under `AEAD.meshRoutedItemV1`, keyed by the manifest's single-use content key, with mesh/item/origin/type token authenticated) and its inverse; since ProximityKit plan step A0.2.6 `seal`, `open` and `additionalData` take `in purposes:` and the label is the host namespace's `purposes.aead.meshRoutedItemV1`. The `FMRI1` marker rides in the clear so a foreign or retired blob is refused BY NAME; the seal bound and the open bound are one derived number, and since **P6 item 3** that number is a FORMULA — `PrivateMediaStore.maxIncomingPhotoBytes` + the body's framed-header allowance + the seal overhead — which is also the registry's photo-row cap, so the seam bound and the per-type cap cannot drift. Pure — no actor, clock, I/O or identity; the one `PrivateMediaStore` import reaches a single `Int`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshRoutedItemBody.swift` | P5 item 13 + **P6 items 4 and 6**: `MeshRoutedItemBodyFormat` and THREE body families — `MeshRoutedPhotoHeader`/`MeshRoutedPhotoBody`, `MeshRoutedTextHeader`/`MeshRoutedTextBody`, and `MeshRoutedHeartHeader`/`MeshRoutedHeartBody` (item 6: header-only, so its cap formula has a ZERO payload term; FOUR hostile shapes, the fourth being a non-empty remainder, because a heart body ends at its header and unread bytes inside an authenticated blob are a malleability seam; plus a day-key shape check and the same 128-byte name bound refused on the wire) — the sealed plaintext of a routed photo, framed as a length-prefixed JSON header plus the image RAW (never base64, which would inflate every byte bound in the phase by a third). **P6 item 3** adds the header allowance (`maxHeaderJSONByteCount` / `maxFramedHeaderByteCount`) a routed type's ciphertext cap reserves — an allowance the cap formula reads, never a new refusal. The text body has FIVE hostile shapes on the one `malformed` token since item 4's fix review: the fifth is a decoded `senderName` above the 128-byte bound, refused on the WIRE and not only bounded at the sender, because the allowance's whole arithmetic is over that number. No `keyEpoch`, no sender-supplied identity. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshRoutedOrigination.swift` | P5 item 13: `MeshRoutedShareSkip`, `MeshRoutedShareRefusal` (public, `CaseIterable` since the P5 review's finding 5) and `MeshRoutedOriginationOutcome` — what the routed SENDER door answers. "Nobody to send to" is a silent skip; "the mint was attempted and failed" is a visible refusal, published as a frozen token on `MeshNetworkManager.routedShareRefusal` and forked into localized copy by the app's `RoutedShareRefusalCopy`. Frozen English tokens, never user copy. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshRoutedRefusalBudget.swift` | The P5 review's finding 3 (closing D-12.14): `MeshRoutedRefusalBudget`, the per-**sender** budget for routed content frames refused BEFORE any store verb — a verifier rejection, `notADestinationOrHandoff`, `unknownItemNotFromOrigin`, `unregisteredTypeChunk`, an undecodable frame — which the replay window cannot record. Counts refusals (never frames) per authenticated envelope sender, capped at `MeshRoutedDrainBounds.sessionFramesPerPeer` (an honest peer cannot reach it: that is already the most frames the drain lets it make this device serve; frames for an item this device already capacity-refused from that sender are exempt, or a courier facing a full receiver would be), bounded on both axes (sender axis = the admission set's 16), fail-closed on a full sender axis, reset with the drain state only. A spent sender's content is dropped at `dispatchRoutedContent` unread after one `mesh.routedDrain.refusalBudgetSpent` line. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshRoutedItemDelivery.swift` | P5 item 13: `MeshRoutedDeliveryError`, `MeshRoutedOriginQuotaKey` and `MeshRoutedItemDelivery` — the ONE routed plaintext seam (unwrap the content key, open the item blob, decode the body), in its own file because the locked-device walls pin the unwrap and open to one home and require it to GUARD on `mayDecryptRoutedContent`. Takes the predicate as a parameter and refuses on it first. **P6 items 4 and 6** add `openTextBody` and `openHeartBody` beside `openPhotoBody` and funnel all three through one private `openPlaintext` whose first line is the guard — structure rather than a wall, because W2's containment half is a whole-FILE `contains` and cannot see a second entry point skipping it. Also holds `MeshRoutedProjectionVerdict` (handed on / refused for good / refused for now — the retry-list decision) and `MeshTranscriptLiveness`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshRoutedRetryPlan.swift` | P6 item 5 (D-13.32): the re-entry pass's allowance discipline, as values — `MeshRoutedRetryList` (the two retry lists as frozen audit tokens), `MeshRoutedRetryPlan` (pure, `nonisolated`, deterministic: never-attempted keys first, the retry share capped at `maxItems / retryShareDivisor`, unused slots spilling either way, the share ordered by a round-robin queue so its own tail cannot starve) and `MeshRoutedRetryRotation` (the memory-only per-list tried set + queue + session cut, bounded by `MeshRoutedStoreFormat.maxItems`, cleared with the drain state, and **pruned to the pass's own enumeration** by `retain(_:)` since item 6's run — a key that left its list by some other door costs no slot but does fill the 1024 bound, and at the bound the pacing silently reverts to the head-of-list prefix D-13.32 exists to stop). Its session cut is armed with a **floored** instant, because the record's `firstSeenAt` is floored at every admission door and comparing the two unfloored charged a genuinely new item to the retry share. It names no routed type token, no registry, no store and no manifest — deliberately, and walled by `MeshRoutedRetryPlanTests.thePlannerNamesNoRoutedTypeNoRegistryAndNoStore`: the per-type opinion belongs to the manager's filter-before-plan seam (`isProjectableAtThisPass`, `ackableNow`), which is where item 6 adds the heart predicate. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshCustodyReceipt.swift` | P5 item 3: `MeshCustodyReceiptFormat`, `MeshCustodyReceipt`, `MeshCustodyReceiptPayload`, `MeshCustodyReceiptMintError` and the mint — a custodian's signed statement that it durably holds one item's COMPLETE ciphertext, signed by the custodian ABOUT the origin's item. The mint takes a `MeshCustodyDurabilityWitness`, so the forbidden order (receipt first, durability later) is unwritable. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshCustodyReceiptVerifier.swift` | P5 item 3: `MeshCustodyReceiptRejection` + `MeshCustodyReceiptVerifier` — the receive-side door, resolving the key by the CUSTODIAN fingerprint from the admission ledger. | `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshRoutedStoreKey.swift` | P5 item 3: `MeshRoutedStorageScope` (directory + its OWN `com.fernlet.mesh-routed` keychain service), `MeshRoutedSealKeyOutcome` and `MeshRoutedSealKey` — the key row that seals the routed index and every chunk file, minted only on a definitive absence and read-back-verified. Since ProximityKit plan step A0.2.8 the scope also carries the host's `namespace`, its production scope and `keychainService(besideHeartDrop:in:)` read the namespace's directory and seal-key service, and the key's reads take the namespace's `account:`. NOT `MeshInventoryDigest` — that is P4's membership summary under a different token. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshRoutedInventoryBuilder.swift` | P5 item 5: `MeshRoutedInventory.init?(meshID:index:selfFingerprint:at:)` — deriving the digest from a LOADED index, read-only and clock-injected, with the custody self-rule (this device appears iff it holds custody of somebody ELSE's item) and the one filter (`isLive(at:)`, so parked, delivered and unrestorable-delivery items are all advertised). Bounds the at-rest `chunkCount` before it becomes a bitmap allocation size — an out-of-range count yields an empty map, so the built digest refuses its own shape check rather than sizing a `Data` off a planted `UInt32`. Adds no store verb and no index enumerator. |

| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshRoutedDrainAnswer.swift` | P5 item 6: the drain's quiescence frame — `MeshRoutedDrainAnswerFormat`, `MeshRoutedDrainAnswer` (mesh, WHOSE advertisement, WHICH one, the single bit), `MeshRoutedDrainAnswerPayload` (+ its own shape check and `@MainActor signed(...)`, which takes no `selfFingerprint`) and `MeshRoutedDrainAnswerMintError`. Token `fernlet.mesh.routed-drain-answer.v1` — its own stem, because the frame states the result of a COMPARISON rather than what a disk holds. Signed, because the bit is a **recorded fact** rather than a gate: item 7's window closes on the membership digest (D-7.11), while this bit is stored per peer, counted onto the merge's audit line and is item 9's capacity input. |

The two BINDING checks (this device is the advertiser; the instant is one it really advertised) live at the manager, which is the only place that knows what was advertised. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshMergeWindow.swift` | P5 item 7: the merge exchange in flight, as an explicit value — `MeshMergeWindow` (three bounded per-peer sets **asked**/**answered**/**matched**, the window's own evidence map, `openedAt` recorded and never compared, and the proof budget), plus `MeshMergeWindowRole`, `MeshMergeWindowClosure` and `MeshMergeWindowVerdict`. The closing law is `pending = (asked ∪ answered) ∩ reachable ∖ matched`; "answered" sits inside `pending`, so answering never closes anything; a peer's later mismatching digest un-matches it, and so does the late re-ask a re-committing peer gets (`reAsking(_:)`, D-7.32) — the opening `asking(_:)` un-matches nobody. Pure, no clock, no I/O — the manager only calls transitions. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshRoutedDrainPlan.swift` | P5 item 6: the drain's pure half — `MeshRoutedPeerInventory` (per-peer state, BOTH quiescence halves), `MeshRoutedDrainRefusalNote` (item 9's visible-failure seam), `MeshRoutedDrainBounds` (64 chunks per answer = 16 MiB, and a per-peer SESSION budget sized for exactly one maximal item), `MeshRoutedDrainChunkSend` and `MeshRoutedDrainPlan`, which re-decides no policy: it filters by the peer's refused set, truncates to the bounds and to the remaining allowance, and keeps the delta's canonical order. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshCustodyHandoffPlan.swift` | P5 item 8 (plan §10.6, §11): custody-transfer-on-departure as pure values — `MeshCustodyHandoffScope` (the run-scoped entitlement a live development opens), `MeshCustodyHandoffSuppression`, `MeshCustodyHandoffResult` (what actually transferred, what could not be placed, what the push offered) and `MeshCustodyHandoffPlan` itself: which items go to which custodian over which legs, plus the custodian-side claim planner and the push batch. No store, no clock, no isolation; every bound is an existing constant. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshRoutedCustodyHandoff.swift` | P5 item 8: the two BATCH custody doors — `recordingCustodyHandoff(_:now:)` (the departing origin, receipt-backed) and `claimingHandedOffLegs(_:now:)` (the custodian, receipt-free) — each one load, N bounded updates and ONE save, plus `MeshRoutedCustodyHandoff`, `MeshRoutedHandoffClaim`, `MeshRoutedHandoffRefusalReason` (the store's vocabulary or the delivery ladder's, so a refusal keeps the name the door that raised it gave it), `MeshRoutedHandoffRefusal`, `MeshRoutedHandoffStep` and `MeshRoutedHandoffReport`. | `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshRoutedCapacity.swift` | P5 item 9 (backpressure): the routed store's caps as ONE injectable value — `MeshRoutedCapacity` (`.production` defined **as** `MeshRoutedStoreFormat`, so no number is written twice; injected via `MeshRoutedStore.init(scope:capacity:)` and read back off the store by everything that accounts), `MeshRoutedCapacityUsage` (the accounting rule stated once — items with parked ones included, staged bytes, files, the over-commit `uncompletableItemCount`, `unrestorableItemCount`, and `hasRoomToAdmit`, the release predicate for a `.storeFull` hold) and `MeshRoutedParkedDrop`, the ONE-clause terminal-refusal rule: `unknownTypeToken` **and** the sender is the set's own claimed origin. Every other manifest rejection keeps the bytes. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshRoutedDeliveryHold.swift` | P5 item 9: the bounded observable behind the user-visible backpressure failure — `MeshRoutedDeliveryHoldCause` (frozen `storeFull` / `notPlaced`; `deferred`, seal-`refused` and `corrupt` are three other answers and set NO hold) and `MeshRoutedDeliveryHold` (cause + count + instant). Counts and tokens only: no display text crosses the module boundary, and no item id reaches the visible surface. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshRoutedAccessGate.swift` | P5 item 10 (locked-device handling, plan §11 + §19.5): item 10's whole pure vocabulary — `MeshRoutedAccessGate` (the three app-level facts that decide whether PLAINTEXT may exist right now: iOS data protection, the app scene, and the app lock's duress session), `MeshRoutedCapability` (`sealCustody` is answered `true` **deliberately** — custody's authority is the store's five states), `MeshRoutedAccessEdge` (the two ciphertext legs rise, duress falls), `MeshRoutedReentryReport` (counts and legs only) and the two frozen token enums the store's suppression lines are named with, `MeshRoutedIndexReadReason` and `MeshRoutedSweepVerb`. | `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshRoutedTypeRegistry.swift` | P5 item 11 (plan §11's "declared at registration"): the routed type-token registry as ONE value — `MeshRoutedTypeEntry` (the five §11 columns plus the derived foreground-decrypt requirement and the declared canonical-store slot), the four frozen column enums `MeshRoutedDestinationSemantics` / `MeshRoutedRelayRetention` / `MeshRoutedExpiryRule` / `MeshRoutedCanonicalStore`, and `MeshRoutedTypeRegistry` itself (+ `increment1(_:)`, the one registry shipping code names, its three rows' tokens read off the routed types it is handed — the manager hands it its host namespace's). `entry(for:) == nil` **is** "unknown", the one answer at every door; `init(entries:)` DROPS a row declaring increment 2's relay-retention or a cap outside the wire bound, so an unimplemented policy is unreachable by construction rather than by comment. **P6 item 3** narrowed the first cap — the photo row now declares `MeshRoutedItemSealFormat.maxResidentBlobByteCount`, which is what makes the manifest door's per-type check reachable. Deliberately holds no gate vocabulary, no store, no clock, no persistence and no raw token literal — the spellings are the host's (Fernlet's in FernletConnections' `RoutedTypes.fernlet`), read in this module only where these rows are built. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshRoutedContentHasher.swift` | P5 item 3: `MeshRoutedContentHasher` — the streaming sibling of `MeshRoutedContentDigest.contentHash(of:in:)`, so a 256 MiB item's whole-item hash is measured one chunk file at a time under the same domain; seeded by `init(purposes:)` since ProximityKit plan step A0.2.6. |

| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Wire/FernletIdentityEnvelope.swift` | Ed25519-signed wire envelope for all peer-to-peer transfers, including canonical JSON encoding and verification. Since ProximityKit plan step A0.2.4 `signed` and `verify` sign and check under their identity's namespace labels, the schema-v1 read under the family's verify-only legacy label (refused when the family refuses legacy peers). Since plan step A0.3 `verify` reads the same namespace's payload rules too, in the checks' fixed order: an unsealed envelope whose token is in `sealingRequired` is refused and one outside `known` is parked (the envelope's own sealing set is gone); a token form of `signed` mints under a token the host names, and `payloadType` / `isUnknownPayloadType` are Fernlet's typed view of the token. |

Since ProximityKit plan step A0.2.4 the token signs under its admitter's namespace labels and `verify` takes the caller's (`in purposes:`), its legacy alternative refused when the family refuses legacy peers. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/FriendPhotoPayloads.swift` | Friend-photo payload, session metadata, manifest, and missing-photo request wire models. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Identity/IdentityService.swift` | Per-device Ed25519 signing identity and X25519 key-agreement, with keys stored in Keychain under `AfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly`. Built from the host's `ProximityNamespace` since ProximityKit plan step A0.2.3: `init(namespace:keychainService:)` (a `nil` service is the namespace's identity service, the literal the retired `init(keychainService:)` default spelled) keeps `namespace` and exposes `purposes`, and `sign`/`verify` gained `ProximityCryptographicPurpose` overloads — `sign` refuses a misframed transcript, a verify-only `.signature(.absent)` label and every non-signature role with `invalidKeyData` — beside the unchanged `CryptographicPurpose` ones. Since A0.2.6 the transport `seal`/`open` and the group-key `encryptGroupKey`/`decryptGroupKey` take their HKDF salts and AEAD labels from `purposes`, and since A0.2.8 the four device rows' accounts are the namespace's `installation.keychain.identity` (`accounts`), which `classifyDeviceIdentityRows` also takes. Since 2026-09-06 (F-1) `ensureProvisioned()` fails CLOSED on an unreadable identity row: the two identity-row reads and Case 3's legacy read distinguish absence (`ProximityKeychainItem.loadDistinguishingAbsence` since A0.2.11, ProximityKit's copy of FernletFoundation's `KeychainItem`), the pure `classifyDeviceIdentityRows` decides, and any status other than not-found throws `IdentityError.keychainReadFailed` rather than minting over — and deleting — a live identity. |

The draft carries the recipe itself, so the text and the Messages card follow the switch. When a share ends it cross-fades to `RecipeShareConfirmationPanel` (latched from the manager's `lastShareOutcome`, announced, success/error haptic) and stays until Done instead of auto-dismissing; a successful send moves radio custody to a hand back that, 1.4 s after the send, runs the same gated stop-and-restart its disappearance would, so an early Done or swipe-down never stops the radio before the sent frame drains. |

Pinned by `RecipeShareConfirmationTests`. |

Since U3: an optional `notNow` (the overlay's header text button, `friends.review.notNow`, also VoiceOver's escape gesture via `EscapePerformsNotNow`; nil in the camera sheet, which keeps its own swipe-down and escape), the title as a heading, tile labels "Photo from <name>" (`PeerNameDisplay`, placeholder `.met`) with a hint, and the `.endingSession` working message. Its keys moved from ProximityKit's `ProximityUICopy` unchanged in plan step A0.1 (28 with `NothingSavedError`'s inline one); `ProximityUICopy` keeps the camera's hold-failure line and the two `PeerNameDisplay` placeholders. |

Plus `ProximityLiveActivityReaper` — the launch-time reaper (called once per process from `FernletStoreLoader.startIfNeeded()` before the store exists) that ends proximity activities a killed previous process stranded; it is the only remaining reader of the attributes type. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/ProximityHost.swift` | `@MainActor` host-protocol seam letting the mesh/recipe-share managers reach app state (display name, trusted peers, trust vault, block/fingerprint checks, nearby-hearts opt-in) without naming concrete `FernletStore`. Since ProximityKit plan step A0.2.3 it also requires `proximityNamespace`, the host's protocol identity, with **no default** in the extension, so a host that supplies none does not compile; the three radio managers read it once at construction. Since A0.2.8 the extension's `proximitySupportDirectory`, `meshSessionStorage` and `meshRoutedStorage` defaults are built from it. Since A0.2.9 it also requires `proximityInstallBinding`, the host's `ProximityInstallBinding`, again with no default, which the two default scopes carry. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Namespace/ProximityCryptographicPurpose.swift` | **ProximityKit plan step A0.2.1**: `ProximityCryptographicPurpose`, ProximityKit's own domain-separation value — one label (`rawValue`, `data`) and the `Role` its namespace field fixes (`signature`/`hashDomain` under a `Framing`, salt, column seal, AAD, TLS exporter label). `prefixBytes` and the positional `signingBytes(_:)` reproduce FernletCrypto's `CryptographicPurpose` byte rules exactly (`.lengthPrefixed` = 8-byte big-endian count + bytes); a non-signature role signs nothing. Internal initializer from a `StaticString`: only the namespace group initializers mint one, so every label is a source literal. Every ProximityKit read of the namespace's 39 labels goes through it (moved there in A0.2.4 to A0.2.9); only the 13 feature labels still read FernletCrypto's registry, until A0.4. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Namespace/ProximityNamespace.swift` | **A0.2.1**: `ProximityNamespace`, the host's protocol identity as one `Sendable` value — `family` (shared by interoperating apps) and `installation` (this app on this device) — with `soundness` computed once by the total `init`, `validated(family:installation:)` (typed `throws(ProximityNamespaceError)`), `labelRows` (every label with its field path, declaration order), and the `LabelRow`, `Soundness`, `Violation` (19 named rules: A0.2's 13, then the vocabulary's four — malformed, duplicate and unknown tokens, malformed summary titles — and the presentation strings' two) and `Collision` values. | `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Namespace/ProximityNamespace+Vocabulary.swift` | **ProximityKit plan step A0.3**: the family's payload vocabulary as `public nonisolated` `Hashable, Sendable` values of plain `String` tokens (wire data, never labels, and never decoded) — `Vocabulary` with `SessionMessages` (the introduction and acknowledgement as `SessionMessage`, payload token and signed summary title; the `Heartbeat`, one token with ping and reply titles), `PayloadRules` (`known`, the tokens the host dispatches, any other parked; `sealingRequired`), `Capabilities` (`known` in order, `wire2`, `assumedForLegacyPeers`), `MembershipRecordKinds` (four) and `RoutedTypes` (photo, temp message, heart, reserved control). | `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Namespace/ProximityNamespace+Soundness.swift` | **A0.2.1**: the rules, run once at `init` in a fixed order — labels (1–255 bytes of 0x21–0x7E, distinct, prefix-free by `Data.starts(with:)`), service types (`_name._udp`, RFC 6335 name, lowercase), ALPNs, heartbeat (1–64 bytes, not `{`), lowercase RFC 3986 scheme, keychain names, single-component storage names distinct ignoring case, log subsystem, then the vocabulary (tokens 1–255 bytes of 0x21–0x7E, capability tokens at most 32 and routed types at most 64, as their receivers accept, a set or list named once; no token repeated within its group; the session tokens and sealing set in `payloads.known`, `wire2` and the legacy assumption in `capabilities.known`; summary titles 1–200 characters) and the radios' presentation strings (instance-name prefixes of `[a-z0-9-]` within the room a 63-byte DNS-SD name leaves before 12 or 16 hex characters, the common name 1–64 bytes of printable ASCII), its bounds Namespace-owned constants `ProximityVocabularyGoldenTests` holds equal to the consumers' — every loop bounded by the namespace's fixed shape or the tokens the host listed; and `familyCollisions(with:)` / `installationCollisions(with:)` for a host's own tests. |

| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletConnections/FernletProtocolNamespace.swift` | **ProximityKit plan step A0.2.2**: `ProximityNamespace.fernlet`, Fernlet's protocol identity, as `nonisolated` statics — `Family.fernlet` (`Purposes.fernlet` with the four `.fernlet` label groups, `Radios.fernlet`, the `fernlet` QR scheme) and `Installation.fernletApp` (the identity and seal-key rows, the storage names, the log subsystem). | `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Support/ProximityAudit.swift` | **ProximityKit plan step A0.2, the audit sink**: `ProximityAuditSink` (a `nonisolated`, `Sendable` protocol with one synchronous `record(_:context:)`) and `ProximityAudit`, the module's only audit entry point — `install(_:)` (public, called once by the host at launch) and the internal `log(_:context:)` with the `context: [:]` default every call site (427 in 26 files at this step) uses. The sink sits in a `nonisolated private static let` `Mutex` the slot owns (Power of 10 R6/R9: no `static var`, no `nonisolated(unsafe)`); `log` reads it under the lock and calls it after releasing it, synchronously, on the caller's executor. No sink installed means the line is dropped: ProximityKit keeps no log and no default. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletConnections/FernletAuditBridge.swift` | **A0.2, the audit sink**: `FernletAuditBridge`, Fernlet's `ProximityAuditSink` — a stateless `public nonisolated struct` whose `record(_:context:)` calls `FernletAuditLog.log(event, context: context)` unchanged and in line (no prefix, no rename, no hop), so the os.Logger output and every `FernletAuditLog.addCaptureHandler` capture see exactly the ProximityKit lines they saw when ProximityKit named `FernletAuditLog`. Installed first thing in `FernletApp.init`, not behind `UITestSupport`. |

Each of its five query dictionaries is built in one place and is FernletFoundation's for the same call, so the two read and clear each other's rows; `delete` and `deleteAll` audit a failure through `ProximityAudit` as `keychain.delete.failed` and `keychain.deleteAll.failed`, FernletFoundation's names and context keys. | `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/HeartSharing/ProximityHeartLedger.swift` | Device-local JSON sidecar for received hearts (Home bubble + 24h health-bar glow) plus the rolling 1-per-friend-per-5-minute rate limit enforced both directions; never synced. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Messaging/SessionMessageStore.swift` | Deliberately non-`Codable`, memory-only holder of the current friend-session chat transcript; sanitizes, caps and dedupes messages and clears them at every session end and formation. **P6 item 4** makes the visible transcript a DERIVATION over a held `MeshContentSet<MeshMergedMessage>` — plan §12's own word — so it reads in §10.3's total order rather than arrival order (a routed backlog drains by origin fingerprint), the 500-row cap evicts in that order, and a gate that closes hides rows without destroying them. `receiveIncoming` answers a three-way `Acceptance` and takes a required `seenAt`; the per-sender token bucket retired with the live transport it belonged to. Every dedup — the seen set, the held `MeshContentSet`, the attribution table and `Message.id` itself — keys on `MeshContentKey` = `(senderFingerprint, id)` since item 4's **fix review**, because the id is the origin's own choice and its manifest crosses in the clear first. A gate flip re-renders at the next INGEST, not at the flip, which the type's own doc now states. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Presence/PresenceEpochPosture.swift` | **P9 item 2 pass 1** (plan §17.1): `PresenceEpochPosture`, the presence radio's ephemeral posture as a pure VALUE — for any instant, the epoch (`IdentityService.presenceEpoch(at:)`, the one presence clock), the service instance name to advertise (the host namespace's frozen presence prefix, Fernlet's `fn-`, plus 64 bits of fresh entropy as lowercase hex, fixed length on every device at every epoch; the prefix and the common name are handed in by `PresenceManager`'s posture mint, which reads them off its namespace) and the TLS identity to present (one `EphemeralMeshTLSIdentity.Minted`, the module's single certificate path — no new cryptographic purpose). `rotated(at:)` returns `self` inside the epoch and an entirely fresh posture across a boundary, so no name and no certificate survives 900 s. The certificate is minted at the EPOCH'S START (`IdentityService.presenceEpochStart(at:)`), never at the mint instant: its `notBefore`/`notAfter` are readable by anyone in range, so an instant-anchored window would stamp the posture with the second that radio came up. Wall-clock anchored on purpose: the tag epoch must be absolute for pairwise derivation to be mutual, a per-launch phase would itself be a rotation-surviving fingerprint, and a global rotation instant maximises the anonymity set. No radio, no task, no timer, no persistence; the clock and the entropy are injected. `PresencePostureError` is its one diagnostic error (never a `LocalizedError`). Pinned by `PresenceEpochPostureTests`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Presence/PresenceAdvertisement.swift` | **P9 item 2 pass 2**: the presence radio's Bonjour TXT vocabulary as pure functions (`v` plus the pairwise-DH tags, chunked across `t`/`t1` so no entry exceeds DNS-SD's 255-byte ceiling — 24 tags are 311 bytes and would have been refused or truncated on the air), plus `PresenceDialHello`, the one frame a dialer writes so the responder can resolve an inbound QUIC connection back to the browsed peer it matched. Pinned by `PresenceAdvertisementTests`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Presence/PresenceManager.swift` | Standing presence radio on `_fernlet-near2._udp` (QUIC, since P9 item 2 pass 2) broadcasting only rotating pairwise-DH tags for nearby-friend recognition under a per-epoch `PresenceEpochPosture`, and driving the on-demand short-lived pairwise heart send/receive handshake; memory-only. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Presence/ClosenessLedger.swift` | Device-local day-granularity per-friend interaction counters feeding the deterministic closeness score and close-slot assignment (with persisted hysteresis); capped, never synced. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Presence/FriendStateCache.swift` | Device-local cache of a friend's fuzzy wellbeing state + avatar appearance captured at the last in-person meet (`CachedFriendState`), with 30-day staleness treatment; never synced. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/RecipeSharing/RecipeShareAdvertisement.swift` | **P9 item 3 pass 2**: the recipe radio's Bonjour TXT vocabulary as pure functions (`v`, `sid`, `mode`, an optional byte-bounded `name`; the owner contributes all but the `sid`, which belongs to the radio and is re-minted with its posture, and every entry is held under DNS-SD's 255-byte ceiling), plus `RecipeShareDialHello` — the one canonical frame a dialer writes so the responder can resolve an inbound QUIC connection back to a browsed peer, which the retired transport gave for free. Pinned by `RecipeShareOverQUICTests`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/RecipeSharing/RecipeShareTransfer.swift` | **P9 item 3 pass 1** (plan §17.1): the recipe-share radio's two transport-losable decisions as pure values, plus the advertised name's bound. `RecipeShareDiscoveryGate` is the pause/resume contract as a total table over (`isRunning`, `isPaused`, `connectionCount`) × five events — discovery moves at exactly two moments (pause on `connectionRegistered`, resume on `connectionsEvicted` with nothing left held, over a RUNNING radio) and at no others; `refreshRequested`, `transportErrorWhileListening` and `stopped` all resolve through the radio's own `stop()`/`start()` rather than through the gate, so the paused flag never has two owners. `RecipeShareTransfer` is one share's state machine — `connecting → verified → sending → sent/failed/cancelled`, `peerVerified` idempotent and `sendBegan` once-only, `completionCount` an oracle (0 or 1, and 1 exactly when `.sent`), and the load-bearing row that a `discoveryPaused`/`discoveryResumed` is accepted in EVERY phase and moves none of them. Its `route` projection reads `MeshTransferStreamTable.route(reliableByteCount:)` off the encoded plaintext (an estimate, NOT a floor — wire2 deflates a body before sealing it, so a compressible plaintext over the floor can still ride the control stream; nothing acts on the projection, the radio routes on the sealed frame's real size): a text-only recipe stays on the control stream, a recipe carrying a picture clears the bulk floor — which is why the pass-2 recipe radio needs the per-transfer-stream acceptor `NetworkPresenceSession` deliberately has none of. `radioIsQuiet` is SEEDED from the radio at the mint, not defaulted — a second share to an already-paired peer is minted between gate transitions and would otherwise claim an open radio for its whole life. `RecipeShareAdvertisedName` caps the advertised name at `MeshLinkAdvertisement.maxFieldValueLength` (64) BYTES on grapheme boundaries, because `publishedFields` drops an over-long value rather than cutting it and 24 CJK Characters are 72 bytes; `publishable(_:)` answers "" for the one input it cannot publish (a single grapheme wider than the bound — combining marks survive sanitizing), which the caller OMITS rather than advertising, and `received(_:hint:)` is the matching receive-side rule that falls back to the peer hint on an absent *and* an empty name. No radio, no clock, no task. Pinned by `RecipeShareTransferTests`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/RecipeSharing/RecipeShareOutcome.swift` | `RecipeShareOutcome` + `RecipeShareFailure`: how one recipe share ended, as frozen tokens the app maps to copy. `sent` (the sealed payload was handed to the transport: never an application receipt) or `notSent` with one of seven causes; only `sendIncomplete` means the other person may still have it. Fresh `id` per publication so a repeated failure is still a change. No display text, no fingerprint. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/RecipeSharing/ProximityRecipeShareManager.swift` | `@MainActor` `@Observable` manager for one-at-a-time in-person recipe sharing: nearby-recipient discovery, a single verified sealed connection, send-state machine, pending inbound shares, and diagnostics. Since **P9 item 3 pass 1** its two transport-losable decisions are tier-1 values it consults rather than inline guard chains: `applyDiscoveryGate(_:)` is the ONLY caller of `pauseDiscovery()`/`resumeDiscovery()` (one each, walled by `RecipeShareTransferTests`), and `applyTransfer(_:)` drives the `RecipeShareTransfer` record — minted in `sendRecipeShare` (seeded from `isDiscoveryPaused`), gating the once-only send start in `sendPendingPayload`, cancelled by the pre-connect timeout, and DISCARDED outright (`transfer = nil`, no `.cancelled`) by `stop()`/`refreshDiscovery()`. The advertised `name` is bounded in bytes by `RecipeShareAdvertisedName` and omitted entirely when it cannot be published; an absent or empty inbound name falls back to the picker's existing "A friend" placeholder, never to a transport token — `displayName(for peer:)` is the ONE rule for naming a peer in anything a user reads (the picker row, the four "Connection details" lines and the cap's status line all go through it). **P9 item 3 pass 2** put it behind the `RecipeShareRadioSession` seam and onto QUIC: no MultipeerConnectivity type is left in the file, `invite` became `dial(_:helloSID:)`, the inbound invitation gate became `resolveDialer` + `shouldAcceptDialer`, `disconnectPeer` became `endTunnel`, and `isListening` stays the radio's own LEVEL account. A failed `start()` goes through the one `handleTransportError(_:)` stand-down door and nothing else does. Each send's outcome is attributed by a **per-send token**, so two overlapping shares to one already-paired peer cannot credit each other's completion. Every share the user begins ends in exactly one published `lastShareOutcome` (`RecipeShareOutcome`) through `finishShare(_:token:)` — the pre-send failures share one `failLiveShare` tail, a connection-record eviction after the pairing came up fails the share as `couldNotConnect` (it used to leave "Connecting to …" stuck), and `stop()`/`refreshDiscovery()` publish `interrupted`/`sendIncomplete` for a share they cut off. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/FriendsDiscoveryEntry.swift` | P6 item 2 fix: the Friends tab's discovery entry as a pure three-way value (`fresh` / `resume` / `none`) over `isInSession × hasCommittedPeer`, plus whether that entry arms the five-minute timeout (an exhaustive `switch`, never `!= .none`, and no `String` rawValue / `CaseIterable` — nothing read either and a rawValue is a frozen-token obligation; fix review P3-9). Extracted from `ContentView.startFriendsDiscovery()`, where both halves were `private` in the app target and reddened nothing when deleted while being item 2's whole user-facing P1 claim. Same precedent and shape as `FriendMintingReview.sessionEndReview`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshSessionPoll.swift` | **P7 item 4**: `MeshNetworkManager.pollSession(now:)` — the ONE public seam that runs the three on-demand consumers in their one order (ceiling → idle lapse → partition) for a live session and nothing for a dead one, returning `MeshSessionPollReport` (flags only; `sessionLiveAfter` is the app timer's stop condition) — and `armSessionCeilingFromAdoptedMeshIfNeeded(now:)`, the joiner-side ceiling arm called at descriptor adoption and at the admission grant, which closes P6 §12.3 finding 3 (a yielding founder, and every proximity joiner, held a mesh with no ceiling to enforce). No timer, no clock, no plaintext, nothing routed. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Mesh/MeshSessionResumePresentation.swift` | **P7 item 5**: the decision half of the resume surface. `MeshSessionResumePresentation` (public: `nothing` / `offerResume` / `previousSessionEnded(_:)` / `previousSessionCouldNotBeReopened`) with the pure, total `presentation(outcome:offersForegroundResume:isInSession:)` over every `MeshSessionRestoreOutcome` case, `MeshSessionEndingPresentation` (the four-way fold of the eight frozen termination reasons — ended, never failed), and `MeshNetworkManager.sessionResumePresentation`, the one public read over the four observation-ignored restore surfaces the app could not see. A deferral or refusal is deliberately silent; a session surface up presents nothing. |

| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Moderation/ModerationBanStore.swift` | Tamper-resistant 30-day designer store-ban clock: reinstall-proof via a dedicated Keychain service and clock-tamper-proof via a `mach_continuous_time` countdown plus a wall-clock high-water ratchet. Reconciles in both directions: applies warranted bans with their salted-tag evidence, and lifts an active ban whose reporters positively withdrew enough of it. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Moderation/ModerationContentHash.swift` | Stable content key an abuse report binds to: order-stable SHA-256 over a sanitized item's artwork (texture grid + slot), so relisting under a new id/name cannot evade a report. |
