| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Moderation/ModerationReportRelay.swift` | One-hop report wire: `SignedModerationReport`/`ModerationReportPayload` models plus verification of each row's Ed25519 signature against the transport-verified sender key (no transitive relay — the Sybil defense). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Wire/ActivityPayloads.swift` | Group Activities wire envelopes plus `schemaVersion`-gated join-token/roster-snapshot signing and verification and the `ActivityParamsHash` descriptor hash. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Wire/CanonicalSignatureSerializer.swift` | Deterministic, cross-platform-stable positional length-prefixed binary canonical-byte serializer for Ed25519 signing, replacing JSON `.sortedKeys` to prevent cross-stack `signatureInvalid`. Since ProximityKit plan step A0.2.4 ten overloads (envelope, admission token, the membership family, the quorum, the key advertisement) and `canonicalInventoryDigestBytes` take `in purposes:` and write their domain from the host's namespace; since A0.2.5 the channel-introduction overload and the six routed ones (manifest, chunk, both receipts, routed inventory digest, drain answer) do too, leaving only the four activity and moderation tags, which move with their features at A0.4. The inventory digest's preimage writes each record identity's kind token, resolved from its family's record kinds when the identity was built. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Wire/MessagePayloads.swift` | `TempMessagePayload` wire value type for a single sealed live-session chat message (id/text/sentAt); receiver sanitizes and length-caps before use. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Wire/RecipeSharePayloads.swift` | `ProximityRecipeSharePayload` wire envelope wrapping a `ProximitySharedRecipe` for in-person recipe sharing, plus the optional 512 KB `imageJPEGData` wire image (cap enforced at the receive door via `droppingOversizeImage()`) and the share-notes / include-picture strips (the notes strip rewrites a multipart partition through `SharedRecipePayload.withoutSteps()`). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Wire/SealedIntroductionEnvelope.swift` | Transport wrapper carrying only the ciphertext of a KA-sealed identity intro/ack for presence-heart handshakes, so a tag-replay forger cannot deanonymize the sender's identity. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Wire/TrainerPayloads.swift` | `TrainerExportPayload` wire envelope carrying the opaque, size-bounded curated Trainer/Nutritionist export bundle; sealing-required so an unsealed send fails closed at verify. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Wire/SealedPayloadFraming.swift` | wire2 sealed-body framing (compress + pad inside the AEAD). Threaded through `IdentityService.seal/open` and envelope verify; derived from the peer's advertised capabilities, never from the bytes alone. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Wire/ProximityVerification.swift` | The QR verification ceremony that upgrades a non-UWB `awaitingManualCommit` slot to ceremony grade: the QR proves the signing key is physically on screen, the sealed challenge/response proves the live peer holds it. Since ProximityKit plan step A0.2.5 the URL scheme and both QR labels are the host namespace's: `makeURL(identity:now:)` reads the signing identity's, `parse(_:in:)` compares a scanned scheme with the caller's namespace, and `isValid(_:at:in:)` and `ProximityVerifySignature.message(...in:)` take the caller's purposes; the static `urlScheme` and both domain constants are gone, the host `verify`, query key `d` and version 1 stay format constants. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Trust/CoachVerificationCeremony.swift` | Slot-independent verification ceremony for the in-person coach session (Increment 10). Carries the nonce-binding rules — per-peer nonce, sign-after-check, wrong-peer drop must not clear. **No production callers yet:** the coach session manager is unbuilt. Since ProximityKit plan step A0.2.5 it scans, answers and checks under its identity's namespace (`identity.namespace`, `identity.purposes`). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/Trust/CoachSessionTrustPolicy.swift` | `CoachSessionContract` (the written-down coach/trainee role split) and the coach trust policy — a coach is NOT a friend, so only an unrevoked `.trainer` vault record with `unknownModeToken == nil` auto-confirms. **No production callers yet.** |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/HeartSharing/HeartDropService.swift` | Offline "away" hearts over the CloudKit public-DB dead-drop. All crypto happens here on the sealed side of the wall; the injected transport only ever sees rotating day tags plus ciphertext. Its `identity` has no default since ProximityKit plan step A0.2.3 — the host passes the identity built from its namespace. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/HeartSharing/HeartDropSealer.swift` | The outer seal for heart drops: versioned wire form with a prekey id (all-zeros = sealed to the static key). Gates payload size *before* key agreement. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/HeartSharing/HeartPrekeyStore.swift` | One-time X25519 prekeys for forward-secret drops, plus the X3DH-style signed prekey. Private halves live in one keychain blob (`AfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly`, never synchronizable). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/HeartSharing/HeartDropPeerBundleCache.swift` | Cached prekey bundles gossiped by friends plus per-friend consumed-prekey marking. A bundle is only ever stored from a verified, signed identity intro, keyed by the sender's full signing key. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/HeartSharing/HeartDropOutbox.swift` | Persisted sender-side queue for offline drops plus the durable receive dedup — two sidecars beside `HeartLedger.json`, deliberately outside the synced snapshot. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/HeartSharing/ProtectedSidecar.swift` | The `ProtectedSidecar` state machine (absent / deferred / corrupt / loaded) and `SidecarSeal`. Fixes the class of data loss where any read failure read as "no data" and the next persist overwrote the real file. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/HeartSharing/HeartDropSidecarKey.swift` | Keychain-backed ChaChaPoly seal for the heart-drop sidecars at rest — the plaintext versions were a timestamped log of who the user sent affection to. One-way plaintext→sealed migration. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/ProximityKit/HeartSharing/HeartDropStorageScope.swift` | The storage identity of one device's heart-drop state — sidecar directory + the keychain service holding the seal key and prekey blob. One value because `wipeForDeleteAll()` destroys both, so isolating either alone isolates nothing. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/VerifyQRViews.swift` | `QRCodeRenderer` plus the verify-QR display/scan sheets — renders this device's signed `fernlet://verify` URL for the ceremony above. |

## Private Media Stores

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateMediaStore/PrivateMediaStore.swift` | Disk-backed mesh photo index with **AES-256-GCM at-rest encryption** of image/thumbnail bytes and of the index itself (`MeshPhotoCache.sealed` under the friend-wall media key; a legacy plaintext `MeshPhotoCache.json` is read once, resealed, then deleted), thumbnail generation, hydration, FIFO eviction (1000 cap / 900 warn), and orphan cleanup. (Formerly `MeshPhotoCacheStore`.) `maxIncomingPhotoBytes` is `public` since **P6 item 3** so the routed mesh path derives its per-type ciphertext cap from this one plaintext bound instead of restating 10 MB. Since 2026-09-30: an index file that EXISTS but cannot be read loads `.deferred` (never as an empty wall a later save would sweep), and `commitKept(_:onto:) -> WallKeepResult` is the per-photo keep commit a review answer uses — it reports only photos whose sealed bytes landed and the committed index names, never evicts a photo it is keeping, removes its own files when the index write fails, and returns the committed wall (`committedWall`) the owner replaces its mirror with. Every index write cuts session participant lists to the wire decode bound and decodes its bytes back before sealing, so an index always reads back. File mechanics live in `FriendPhotoCorpusFiles`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateMediaStore/FriendPhotoCorpusFiles.swift` | Internal `FriendPhotoCorpusFiles`: the file half both friend-photo corpora share — sealed full-size write reporting whether it landed (`ImageWrite`), the decompression-bomb checks, ImageIO thumbnails, sealed reads (legacy plaintext only where allowed), and the orphan sweep. The wall passes its names/purposes with legacy plaintext allowed; the pending corpus passes its own, born sealed. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateMediaStore/PendingSessionPhotoStore.swift` | `PendingSessionPhotoStore` + `PendingSessionPhotoIndex` / `HeldSessionPhoto` / `HeldPhotoKey` / `AnsweredSessionPhoto`: the sealed corpus of session photos nobody has chosen yet (`PendingSessionPhotos/`, excluded from backup, device-bound key, three `privatePendingSessionPhoto*V1` purposes). Identity by origin + item id with a separate local id for files; hold refused past 200 (never an eviction); answers are one index write with 24 h tombstones (cap 1024); no timer ever removes a held photo; deferred on an unreadable/keyless/newer-format index, purged only on an AEAD failure; `answering(_:in:now:)` is the pure successor an owner adopts when an answer's write fails; `readImage(for:)` → `HeldImageRead` tells a photo that is GONE (missing, or not opening under a present key) from one that cannot be read RIGHT NOW (no key, a file read that failed), so a keep never deletes a ticked photo on a transient failure; every index write reads back by construction (participant lists cut to the decode bound, bytes decoded before sealing); orphan sweep on every clean load; keyless `purgeAll()` for delete-all. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateMediaStore/PrivateMediaKeyStore.swift` | `PrivateMediaKeyProviding` + the keychain-backed AES key provider, split per `Role`: `.friendWall` = the original backup-restorable `AfterFirstUnlock` row (`…contentKey`, unchanged), `.ownPhotos` = a second row (`…ownContentKey`) for the user's own meal/recipe/progress media, whose device binding is gated by `OwnPhotoKeyBinder`, and (2026-09-30) `.pendingSessionPhotos` = a third row (`…pendingContentKey`) for the pending session-photo corpus, born `AfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly`. All three live under `com.fernlet.private-media`, the service the duress wipe sweeps. Fails closed (no mint) when the keychain read is *unreadable* rather than definitively absent. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateMediaStore/OwnPhotoCorpusLayout.swift` | The one home of the own photo corpora's on-disk names (`OwnPhotoCorpusLayout`) and the location set that walks them (`OwnPhotoSealedLocations`). Was `OwnPhotoKeyMigration.swift` and also held `OwnPhotoKeyMigrator` + `OwnPhotoMigrationLatch`; both were retired at the close of the crypto standardization round (owner decision 2026-08-29) once Phase 3's deletion of the unmarked at-rest read left the re-seal pass with no reachable input, and `MediaAtRestFormatMigrationLatch` inherited its half of `OwnPhotoKeyBinder`'s binding gate. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateMediaStore/OwnPhotoKeyBinding.swift` | The own-photo key's device-binding gate (Phase 5, step 5c): `OwnPhotoKeyBinder` (refuses unless the migration latch is set AND the user has a cross-device route, then re-binds the row in place with `SecItemUpdate` — never delete-then-add, which would open a window with no own-photos key at all), `OwnPhotoDeviceBindingConsent` (the recorded, one-way user acceptance for the escrow-off branch), and `OwnPhotoKeyBindingOutcome`. Also the keychain accessors that read and set the row's accessibility class. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateMediaStore/MediaAtRestCrypto.swift` | Shared AES-256-GCM at-rest helpers on `PrivateMediaKeyProviding` (`gcmSeal`, `gcmOpen`, `sealAndWrite` — the last writes nothing at all on a nil key or seal failure, so plaintext never reaches disk). The one home of the seal / open / seal-then-write idiom `PrivateMediaStore`, `MealPhotoStore`, and `ProgressPhotoStore` each hand-rolled; deliberately policy-free, so each store keeps its own fail-closed decision (and its legacy-plaintext branch) at the call site. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateMediaStore/FriendPhotoImageHelpers.swift` | `UIImage` resizing helper for friend-photo sharing. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateMediaStore/MealPhotoStore.swift` | `MealPhotoStore`: on-device AES-256-GCM-sealed store for meal and other private photos with bounded downscale. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateMediaStore/ProgressPhotoStore.swift` | `ProgressPhotoStore`: sealed gym progress-photo timeline over `MealPhotoStore` bytes plus a GCM-sealed dated index. |

## Widgets, Live Activities, And App Intents

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/FernletWidgetsBundle.swift` | `@main` `WidgetBundle` registering the companion widget (mood glyph + water, systemSmall/systemMedium/Lock Screen; emotion faces on the Home Screen families only, a timeline entry at every emotion transition) and the guided-workout and cooking Live Activities. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/WidgetSharedModels.swift` | Deliberately duplicated app-group models (`WidgetSnapshot`, `PendingWidgetAction`, `WidgetCompanionState`, `WidgetCompanionEmotion`, `WidgetEmotionTimeline`, day-key/JSON helpers) so the standalone widget extension avoids linking FernletKit. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/WaterPlusOneIntent.swift` | `AppIntent` behind the companion widget's interactive "+1 water" button; queues a pending action and reloads the widget timeline. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/WorkoutLiveActivity.swift` | Widget-target `Widget` rendering the Lock Screen card and Dynamic Island for the guided-workout rest-timer Live Activity. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/WorkoutActivityAttributes.swift` | `ActivityAttributes`/`ContentState` contract for the guided-workout Live Activity, compiled into both the app and widget targets. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/GuidedWorkoutRunState.swift` | Codable/Hashable value type for an in-progress guided workout, mirrored into the app-group container so Lock Screen intents can advance it. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/AppGroupRunStateStore.swift` | `AppGroupRunStateStore` + `AppGroupRunStatePersistable` — the generic `NSFileCoordinator`-guarded app-group reader/writer behind both run-state files (ISO-8601 + sorted-keys codecs, atomic protected writes, nil-tolerant reads that treat a missing/corrupt/still-protected file as "no active run", and the `updatedAt` re-stamp the app's reconcile ages out on). The single home of the plumbing `GuidedWorkoutRunStateStore` and `CookingRunStateStore` each carried; self-contained (literal app-group id) so it compiles identically into both targets. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/GuidedWorkoutRunStateStore.swift` | The guided-workout specialization of that store — now a `typealias` for `AppGroupRunStateStore<GuidedWorkoutRunState>`; the persistence seam between the app's in-app transitions and the Lock Screen intent runner. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/GuidedWorkoutActivityBridge.swift` | Shared bridge (app + widget targets) that syncs a `GuidedWorkoutRunState` onto the live workout Activity, updating or ending it. Remains the documented seam, now delegating the loop to `LiveActivityReflector`. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/LiveActivityRunReflecting.swift` | `LiveActivityRunReflectable` + `LiveActivityReflector` — the one generic update/end engine (update while the run is live, end on terminal) that `GuidedWorkoutActivityBridge` and `CookingActivityBridge` delegate to instead of each carrying the loop. Every call is parameterized by its own `ActivityAttributes` type, so ending one activity kind can never enumerate the other's instances. Compiled into both the app and widget targets. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/GuidedWorkoutLiveActivityIntents.swift` | `LiveActivityIntent`s for the guided-workout Live Activity's "Done set" and "Skip rest" Lock Screen buttons. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/WidgetBridge.swift` | `WidgetSnapshot` + bridge — app side of the FernletWidgets app-group JSON bridge: outbound benign snapshot and inbound pending widget actions (deliberately S3-walled twin types). **P10 item 4** adds the CONTENT/METADATA split the background refresh's "reload timelines only on change" rests on: `WidgetSnapshot.contentEquals(_:)` compares the six fields that decide what the widget DRAWS and ignores `computedAt`, which is stamped `Date()` at every construction — so the synthesised `==` is false between two snapshots of an unchanged day and a diff built on it would reload the timelines forever. The doc comment carries the per-field table; `WidgetSnapshotContentEqualityTests` pins it, including a `Mirror` field-count pin that reds when a field is added without a classification. `WidgetSnapshotMirror.publishIfContentChanged(_:)` is the handler's publish door — it always WRITES (the stamp moves) and reloads only on a content change, answering `WidgetSnapshotPublication`. `publish(_:)` is untouched and still reloads unconditionally: that scoping is the foreground's, every one of whose callers is a change by construction, and a cell pins it. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/CompanionRefresh/CompanionRefreshIdentifier.swift` | **P10 item 2** (plan §16.4, §17.2): `CompanionRefresh` — the companion background refresh's namespace, holding its one frozen `BGAppRefreshTask` identifier `MBO.Fernlet.companion-refresh` and nothing else (item 3 registers it and adds the `Info.plist` entries; item 4 is the handler). `nonisolated`, because the app target defaults to MainActor isolation and an identifier that needs an `await` to read is one the registration site cannot use — the same reason `MeshContinuationTaskHost.identifierPrefix` is spelled that way. It exists in its own directory on purpose: `App/Fernlet/CompanionRefresh/` is the unit `BackgroundRefreshBoundaryTests` walks, so items 3 and 4 are inside §16.4's import wall from the moment their files land, and this literal is that wall's positive needle — an emptied directory reds it instead of passing vacuously. Writes nothing, so the wipe wall is owed no disposition row. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/CompanionRefresh/CompanionRefreshScheduling.swift` | **P10 item 3** (plan §17.2, §27.1): the companion refresh's OWN `BackgroundTasks` seam — `CompanionRefreshScheduling` (register / submit), `CompanionRefreshTaskHandle` (expiration handler, complete) and the `CompanionRefreshRequest` value, plus the production conformers `SystemCompanionRefreshScheduler` and `SystemCompanionRefreshTaskHandle`. A SECOND seam rather than a widened `MeshContinuationScheduling`, deliberately: two tasks through one protocol is how a fake stops modelling either, and the mesh's members (progress, a two-sentence card, four ending reasons) have no meaning for an app refresh. Three places the mesh's shape did NOT transfer, each with its reason in the header: no pre-register cancel (the refresh identifier is process-stable, so a request from a previous launch is exactly what this one wants delivered — cancelling would break the chain every cold start, silently), no pre-submit cancel (the pending request IS the fallback when a submission throws), and no `strategy` / `progress` / `updateTitle` (`BGAppRefreshTaskRequest` has none of them; `earliestBeginDate` is the only thing there is to ask for). It is the app's only file that names `BGAppRefreshTask`, which `MeshContinuationTaskHostTests` cell (a) pins in both directions alongside the mesh's `BGContinuedProcessingTask`. The conformers are the untested remainder and are item 9's device row — including §27.2's open measurement of what a Simulator does with an app-refresh submission at all. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/CompanionRefresh/CompanionRefreshCoordinator.swift` | **P10 item 3**: `CompanionRefreshCoordinator` — the process-global (`.shared`, an immutable `static let` in `FernletStoreAccess.shared`'s idiom) that owns the companion `BGAppRefreshTask`: one registration at launch, the two submission triggers, and exactly-once completion of whatever the system delivers. **The schedule policy is at handle + background, never on a timer** (plan §26.3/§27.3): trigger (a) is the tail of every handler run, which submits the successor BEFORE it completes the task, and trigger (b) is the `.background` scene edge — there is no third, and no clock at all (no `Timer`, no `DispatchQueue`, no `Task.sleep`; the app owns exactly one timer and it is `ProximitySessionPoller`'s). **The edge asks only when nothing is pending**, held in an in-memory `pendingRequest` slot: `BGTaskScheduler` REPLACES a same-identifier request, so an unconditional edge submission slid the floor fifteen minutes further out on every switch away and a person who opened the app more often than that would never be delivered one. A delivery empties the slot, a refusal leaves it empty, and the tail is unconditional because it runs after a delivery; the accepted cost of an in-memory slot is one slide per process launch. The R2 cap (`maxEdgeSubmissionsPerLaunch`) bounds the EDGE alone — the tail is exempt, because a never-reset lifetime budget shared with it would end the chain permanently on a process iOS keeps suspended for days. `earliestBeginInterval` is 15 minutes: a floor the app states, not a cadence it gets, and a constant so a soak can move it with a measurement. **Item 3's handler is a placeholder by design** — adopt, submit the successor, complete once — so §17.2's seven steps land in item 4 as an insertion between two existing lines rather than a rewrite, and so §16.4's import wall and the exactly-once contract are both already true of the directory before any work runs inside it. Writes nothing: no `UserDefaults`, no "last refreshed at", nothing on disk, so the wipe wall is owed no disposition row. Speaks the seam's protocols and never `BackgroundTasks` itself. **2026-09-21 (item 2c):** the edge budget is charged AFTER `submitNext(trigger:)` reports the ask reached the seam, so a launch whose registration was refused spends none of it and its audit trail keeps naming the refusal instead of the cap (plan §17.2.3 finding 3); `edgeSubmissions <= submissions` is now an invariant. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/CompanionRefresh/CompanionRefreshPipeline.swift` | **P10 item 4** (plan §17.2, §27.3): the companion refresh HANDLER, written as a pipeline over VALUES — `CompanionRefreshStep` (the trace), `CompanionRefreshOutcome` (what the system is told), `CompanionRefreshRun` (trace + verdict + the two facts worth reporting), `CompanionRefreshSteps` (the five post-acquisition steps as `@MainActor` closures) and `CompanionRefreshPipeline` itself. Closures rather than a `FernletStore` parameter because a handler that named the store could be driven in a unit test only by BUILDING one, which is the single thing §17.2 forbids outright; acquire is two-phase (it returns the remaining steps rather than a store) so the store appears in no type here at all. The order is the claim: acquire → inspect the widget action queue → roll the day → recompute the companion → publish through the diff → reload only on a change. A queued widget action ends the run at the inspection with a named outcome and NO publish (decision D-10.4.2: the widget's own "+1 water" App Intent has already written a provisional count into the same file, and republishing the app's lower one would make the count go backwards; §17.2 forbids draining here, and the roll is skipped with the publish because a warm process's roll publishes on its own). Cancellation is checked at the two places it can land — before the acquisition and immediately after it, the pipeline's one suspension point — and nowhere else, because the tail is synchronous main-actor work a cancellation cannot cut into. Never throws and never traps: a background handler that threw would leave the coordinator owing the system a completion, so every failure is a value. Writes nothing and reads no clock; it builds no store of its own, and the acquisition it calls builds the PROCESS's first one on a cold wake, which is what §17.2 allows and what the item-4 verify corrected three places for saying otherwise. **Item 4's verify fixes added the two refusals and the roll's own reload.** The queue skip now yields to a new day (D-10.4.8: one undrained row used to suspend every background publish until the next foreground, and across midnight a snapshot left on yesterday's `dateKey` makes `WidgetDayGate` blank the companion — a widget a tap behind beats a blank one, reported as `publishedDespitePendingActions`). A scoring adjustment switched on without its bridge ends the run at a new `inspectScoringContext` step (D-10.4.6: the bridges are attached only by `ContentView`, so a COLD wake recomputes a score missing the period and stress adjustments, and the handler refuses to publish a number the app itself disagrees with — `scoringContextUnavailable`). And the snapshot file is read either side of the roll, because `refreshCurrentDayIfNeeded()` publishes through the store's own mirror on an advance and that write used to satisfy the handler's diff before it ran, leaving `dayAdvanced` true with no reload in the trace on the most consequential refresh of the day. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/CompanionRefresh/CompanionRefreshWiring.swift` | **P10 item 4**: the ONE place the pipeline's abstract steps are bound to the real app, and therefore the only file in the refresh directory that names anything of the app's. Eight one-expression bindings — `FernletStoreAccess.shared.load()` (the sanctioned acquisition, which refuses before first unlock, hands back the CACHED store, and on a COLD wake builds the process's first one inside the grant), `hasUndrainedWidgetActions`, `publishedWidgetSnapshotIsForCurrentDay`, `hasCompleteScoringContext`, `publishedWidgetSnapshot()`, `refreshCurrentDayIfNeeded()`, `companionState.rawValue`, `currentWidgetSnapshot()` and `ensureWidgetSnapshotMirror().publishIfContentChanged(_:)` — each on `BackgroundRefreshBoundaryTests`' PERMITTED list, so a reviewer can read the body in one screen and decide whether the handler reaches anything it must not. `steps(for:)` is split out of `productionPipeline()` so those five expressions are reachable from a test over an ordinary test store rather than only through the process-global cache. The header records what the day roll and the recompute were TRACED to touch, since the wall cannot see inside them: repository reads and writes only — no HealthKit call, no radio, no forced CloudKit sync (the Core Data stack's own mirroring to the user's private database is ambient, not a sync this handler asks for), and `day.healthContext` is values HealthKit wrote into the diary on an earlier foreground. Imports `FernletDomainModel` for one member, `CompanionState.rawValue`, which member-import visibility makes explicit. Item 4's verify fixes write down what ensuring the mirror COSTS (decision D-10.4.7, accepted): in a process woken purely for background work the mirror outlives the refresh, so any later save there — a Siri water intent — now reloads the widget unconditionally, which was impossible before and is exactly what the foreground already does. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/WorkoutLiveActivityController.swift` | `WorkoutLiveActivityController` — app-side requester for the guided-workout Live Activity (starts one, ends any stale one; degrades silently when Live Activities are disabled). |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/LiveActivityStarter.swift` | `LiveActivityStarter.start` — the shared generic requester behind `WorkoutLiveActivityController` and `CookingLiveActivityController`: ends any already-live activity of the same attributes type, then requests one, and no-ops when the user hasn't enabled Live Activities (request failures degrade silently). App-target only and deliberately outside the dual-membership set, because only the app process can start an activity — unlike the update/end engine (`LiveActivityRunReflecting.swift`), which the Lock Screen intents also need. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/FernletNavigation.swift` | App navigation enums (`FernletTab`, `FernletSheet`) — split out of the design system when it moved into the FernletUI package target (`FernletSheet` references app-resident `FirstAidTool`). Also the re-tap-the-active-tab router: `TabReselectAction` (pop to the tab's main page when something is pushed — after the shared discard alert when a pushed page holds typed input — else scroll to top), `TabReselectModifier` / `.tabReselect(...)`, attached outside each tab page's `NavigationStack`, and the draft half: `TabDraftRegistry` (weak `TabDraftLease`s, injected into the tab's stack) fed by `TabReselectDraftModifier` / `.tabReselectDraft(isDirty:)` on pushed editors. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/FernletAppIntents.swift` | App Intents — `LogWaterIntent` (background app-group queue append, no app launch), `LogMealIntent` / `OpenJournalIntent` (open the app to the matching sheet via a persisted deep-link). |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/FernletShortcuts.swift` | `FernletShortcuts` (`AppShortcutsProvider`) — surfaces the log-water/log-meal/open-journal App Intents to Siri and Spotlight with natural phrases. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/ExchangeIntentService.swift` | `ExchangeIntentService` — the main-process dependency behind the recipe / workout-plan exchange App Intents, plus the entities those intents speak in (`RecipeEntity`, `PlannedWorkoutEntity`, their `EntityQuery`s, `WorkoutPlanImportPreview`, the duplicate/collision policy enums, `ExchangeIntentLimits`, and `ExchangeIntentServiceError`). It creates no repositories and owns no store of its own: the scene loader (via `loadStoreForUI`) and every background intent coalesce through `FernletStoreAccess`. A workout-plan preview carries a calendar-revision fingerprint and an expiry, so an approved plan cannot be applied against a calendar that changed underneath it, and both import paths consult `ExchangeImportLedger` so a retried automation replays its result instead of importing a second copy. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/CookingActivityAttributes.swift` | ActivityKit contract for the cooking Live Activity (recipe name + per-step `ContentState`), compiled into both targets. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/CookingLiveActivity.swift` | The cooking Live Activity widget: Lock Screen card plus every Dynamic Island presentation. Registered in `FernletWidgetsBundle` alongside the workout activity. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/CookingActivityBridge.swift` | The single seam reflecting a `CookingRunState` onto the live activity — shared by the in-app walker and the Lock Screen intents. Delegates its loop to `LiveActivityReflector`. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/CookingLiveActivityIntents.swift` | `LiveActivityIntent`s for the cooking activity's "Next"/repeat-step buttons and the matching Siri phrases; `CookingIntentRunner` performs them in the app's process and posts the notification `FernletStore` reconciles on. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/CookingRunState.swift` | A cooking session as a flat value that survives process death in the app group, carrying the full ordered `steps` so a cold-launched intent can render the next step with no live app state. Each step may carry its multipart `partName` (optional key); `currentPart` and the Live Activity text ("Lemon dressing · …") read it. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/CookingRunStateStore.swift` | Coordinated app-group reader/writer for `CookingRunState.json` — the persistence seam between `FernletStore` and the Lock Screen/Siri intent runner. |

## Messages Extension

The only UIKit target Fernlet ships, and a two-process boundary: the appex reads an App Group
catalog the app publishes and writes hand-off inboxes the app drains. It links exactly one
FernletKit product (`FernletExchange`) and no repository — `MessagesExtensionBoundaryTests` scans
the whole directory to keep it that way.

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletMessagesExtension/FernletMessagesViewController.swift` | `FernletMessagesViewController` — the `MSMessagesAppViewController` composer and received-item viewer. Reads only the bounded App Group catalog, and independently decodes every received envelope rather than trusting the visible message card. Draws its own 1200×630 card artwork locally, so Messages never fetches or exposes a private food photo. Persists nothing of its own since 2026-09-23 (the last-picked-recipe key it kept in its own `UserDefaults.standard`, which the wipe could not reach, was removed). Since 2026-09-30 an opened card is drawn from its own URL alone — `FernletMessagesReceivedItem.resolve` decides the screen, and only the composer reads the catalog (`viewDidLoad` shows an opening state instead of building the composer, and only while no activation has decided yet, so a card drawn before the view loaded is never reset) — held by `MessagesExtensionBoundaryTests.aReceivedCardIsDrawnWithoutTheComposersCatalog`. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletMessagesExtension/FernletMessagesCopy.swift` | `FernletMessagesCopy` — the target's copy vault (the `FernletUICopy`/`FernletLockCopy` model). Every sentence the extension shows, keyed and catalogued, with computed members so the lookup happens under the locale in force at use time — except what a recipe card carries, which moved to `FernletMessagesCardCopy` on 2026-09-30. NO `bundle:` argument: an appex's `Bundle.main` is its own. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletMessagesExtension/FernletMessagesCard.swift` | `FernletMessagesCard` + `FernletMessagesPalette` (2026-09-30) — the ONE builder of a Fernlet Messages card: the envelope's `messageURL()`, the recipe template layout (name and counts read off the envelope's validated card), the 1200×630 artwork and the summary line. ALSO a member of the Fernlet app target (a membership exception on this synchronized folder), so the composer's inserted card and the app's "Send in Messages" card are the same bytes. Imports only the extension's four permitted modules. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletMessagesExtension/FernletMessagesCardCopy.swift` | `FernletMessagesCardCopy` (2026-09-30) — the eight strings a recipe card carries (captions, "Opens in Fernlet on iPhone", the wordmark, the summary line, the three plural-ruled counts), keys unchanged from `FernletMessagesCopy`. Compiled into the app too, where `catalog` is the embedded extension bundle (`PlugIns/FernletMessagesExtension.appex`), so both processes read the extension's catalog and the keys and the counts' plural blocks live there only; `Scripts/sync-string-catalogs.sh` keeps them out of the app catalog's harvest. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletMessagesExtension/Localizable.xcstrings` | The target's string catalog, added 2026-08-27 — the target shipped a round without one, so `String(localized:)` there would have harvested nothing. Synced by `Scripts/sync-string-catalogs.sh`; the four count keys carry hand-authored plural variations. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletMessagesExtension/Assets.xcassets` | The `iMessage App Icon` set App Store Connect requires of an iMessage app (all twelve Xcode slots, including the 1024×768 Messages App Store image), wired through `ASSETCATALOG_COMPILER_APPICON_NAME`. Real art since 2026-09-24 (owner-approved; it replaced the 2026-09-23 placeholder): the Home Screen icon's fern sprig, turned onto the 4:3 diagonal and fitted inside the superellipse the Messages "+" menu clips it to. Rendered by `Scripts/render-imessage-icon.py`, which is the set's source of truth — never hand-edit the PNGs. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletMessagesExtension/PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy` | The appex's own privacy manifest (App Store Connect checks each executable against its own bundle's manifest). Declares no tracking and no required-reason API — held to the code compiled into the binary by `MessagesExtensionBoundaryTests`, and pinned for tracking flags by `NoTrackingBoundaryTests`. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletMessagesExtension/MessageTransportProbe.swift` | Phase-0 physical-device probe proving `MSMessage.url` can carry opaque bytes. DEAD CODE — zero call sites anywhere, including tests. Delete it or wire it up. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletExchange/FernletExchange.swift` | The exchange core: `RecipeExchangePacket` (format version 1 for a one-part recipe — byte-for-byte what every earlier build wrote — and version 2 for a recipe made in parts, whose `contentHash` pre-image carries its version and the parts; each version may carry only its own shape), `WorkoutPlanExchangePacket`, `ExchangeCardMetadata`, `ExchangeLimits`, and `ExchangeMessageEnvelope` — the `data:` URL payload carried by `MSMessage.url`, written as version 2 (the packet's raw JSON, deflated and base64url-encoded once — see `ExchangeMessageWireV2.swift`) and read in both versions; the version-1 envelope still re-derives the card and requires equality, which is what makes a tampered visible card unusable. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletExchange/ExchangeMessageWireV2.swift` | `ExchangeMessageWireV2` — the version-2 Messages wire (2026-09-24): the four-byte frame (magic `F`, version 2, the document's `UInt16` length) around a raw-DEFLATE JSON document, the strict unpadded-base64url codec, and the bounded inflate — the declared length is checked against `ExchangeLimits.maxMessageDocumentBytes` before a byte is inflated, becomes the inflater's hard ceiling, and must match exactly. `OutputFilter` on both sides, so no unsafe pointer and no Power-of-10 allowlist entry. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletExchange/FernletMessagesCatalog.swift` | `FernletMessagesCatalog` — the versioned App Group document the app publishes for the extension: prebuilt packets plus picker metadata, capped at 100 recipes / 100 workouts / 1 MiB. Also `FernletMessagesRecipePicker` / `FernletMessagesWorkoutPicker` (the compact/search rules) and `FernletMessagesCatalogFileStore`. Bounded by construction: browsing it can never widen into repository access. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletExchange/FernletMessagesInbox.swift` | `FernletMessagesInboxStore` and `FernletMessagesInboxLink` — the recipe hand-off inbox. The extension enqueues an opaque record and opens a deep link; the app decodes and presents it for review. Records expire and the queue refuses overflow, so a post-wipe message cannot reopen old content. Cleared by `FernletStore.clearMessagesImportInboxes` — see [PrivacyWipeCoverage.md](PrivacyWipeCoverage.md). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletExchange/FernletMessagesWorkoutInbox.swift` | `FernletMessagesWorkoutInboxStore` / `FernletMessagesWorkoutInboxRecord` — the workout-plan twin of the recipe inbox, carrying the sender's suggested start day for revalidation against the recipient's calendar. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletExchange/FernletMessagesReceivedItem.swift` | `FernletMessagesReceivedItem` (2026-09-30) — what the Messages extension shows for a card someone opened, decided from the card's URL alone: `.recipe`, `.workoutPlan` (already the review-inbox record it will be handed on as) or `.invalid`. Total and non-throwing, so no input yields an empty screen; reads no file, so opening a card never touches the App Group catalog. Moved out of the view controller so `MessagesReceivedItemTests` can pin it. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/ExchangeImportLedger.swift` | `ExchangeImportLedger` — the app-side replay ledger keyed on packet ID + content hash, so the same shared item forwarded twice is recognized as one import rather than duplicated. |

## Share Extension

| `Fernlet/App/FernletShareExtension/ShareViewController.swift` | Share-extension entry `UIViewController` that extracts the shared URL and enqueues it via `SharedRecipeImportQueueWriter`. |

A deliberate hand-copied twin of `AppServices/SharedRecipeImportQueue.swift` (the extension links no FernletKit products); field list, coder config, container fallback chain, and `NSFileCoordinator` coordination must stay in sync — enforced by `SharedRecipeImportQueueMirrorTests`. |

| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/BackgroundRefreshBoundaryTests.swift` | **P10 item 2** (plan §16.4): the background-refresh import wall — the mechanical half of §17.2's "never: mesh, HealthKit, CloudKit force-sync, Foundation Models, store creation". Walks `App/Fernlet/CompanionRefresh/` by DIRECTORY rather than by file name (P9's lesson: an anchor needle over four hand-listed files is a wall over four files), so items 3 and 4 are covered before their files exist. Its claims: a module allowlist of six names, with every one of the 27 FernletKit targets classified forbidden-or-permitted-with-a-reason and a walk of `Package.swift` that reds when a new module has no disposition; an 84-spelling call zero-list — the mesh/HealthKit/CloudKit/FoundationModels types, the ten radio verbs `ProximityRunSeamsTests` counts, the store-creation spellings, the `internal` `FernletStore` doors a second file in the app target can actually speak, and (added by item 3's verify) the 15-row clock-and-persistence family, which is where "schedule at handle + background, never on a timer" and "no new persisted surface" are actually enforced: the scheduling suite had carried those needles over three hand-named paths, which is a wall over three files rather than over the directory item 4's handler lands in; the identifier as a positive needle, exactly once, as code; a floor on the walk; and two drift cells — the ten copied radio verbs against the spellings that file still speaks, and all 37 app-declared needles against the declarations `App/Fernlet/` actually makes — so a rename cannot leave a needle nothing can contain. Item 4's verify fixes RETIRED one: `attachedHealthKitService` had zero production callers (four test assertions and nothing else), so a door that hands out the HealthKit gateway to any file able to name the store is gone from `FernletStore` and `HealthSyncCoordinator` together with its needle, and the three read-only doors the handler gained in its place — `hasCompleteScoringContext`, `publishedWidgetSnapshot()`, `publishedWidgetSnapshotIsForCurrentDay` — are on the PERMITTED list with the argument for each. The call list is the half that holds: the app target is one module, so `store.applyProximityRunPolicy(…)`, `store.meshNetworkManager`, `store.refreshWorkoutsFromHealth()` and `FernletStore.load(…)` all reach the prohibited thing with no import line at all, and an import-only wall is green over every one of them. ACCESS LEVEL is the second gate: the list's first shape named only the PRIVATE funnels (`runProximityPolicy`, `applyProximityRunPolicyFromView`, `healthSyncCoordinator`), which no second file can speak, and the verify caught it passing a probe that spoke the reachable ones — those three are kept as belt behind the internal braces. The line matcher keeps string INTERPOLATION bodies, because `"policy: \(store.reapplyProximityRunPolicy())"` runs the funnel exactly as the bare call does, and a table cell pins the stripper input-by-input. Reuses `S3BoundaryTests.importedModules` / `.containsAtIdentifierBoundary` so the two walls cannot disagree. Wall-load-bearing; gated on the `s3-grep` CI step. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/CompanionRefreshSchedulingTests.swift` | **P10 item 3**: the refresh scheduling seam driven end to end through two fakes, because nothing can make iOS deliver a `BGAppRefreshTask` to a unit test and plan §27.2 is explicit that the Simulator's answer to an app-refresh SUBMISSION is an open measurement rather than the continued-processing path's known `BGTaskSchedulerErrorDomain` 1. Claims the app's half: one registration with exactly the frozen identifier (a drift from `Info.plist` is an error nowhere — iOS just stops delivering); the request built to the 15-minute policy; the chain re-submitting on every handler run, read AT the completion so the order is pinned and not just the count; a refused registration and a refused submission each REPORTED, never swallowed, because an audit line is the only observable a dead chain ever produces; the pending-slot rule in both directions (three edges with no delivery ask ONCE, at the first edge's floor; the tail's request silences the next edge; a refused tail leaves nothing pending and the next edge asks again); the R2 EDGE bound, driven through refusals because with the slot rule that is the only shape an edge storm has, and its twin — the tail re-submitting through 65 deliveries with the edge budget spent; and **exactly-once completion as a five-row table** in P8's oracle shape, covering the expiry door and the system's own installed handler each once and twice, with the sixth order (an expiration landing while the task is still HELD, before the tail) as its own cell because it needs the re-entrant fake. Plus the boundary pins: the mesh's two protocols gained no member — their brace-matched bodies are FROZEN as normalised strings, because the first shape counted the word `func` and stayed green over a `var` requirement — `BGAppRefreshTask` has one home and `BGContinuedProcessingTask` is not it, the clock-and-persistence family still exists on `BackgroundRefreshBoundaryTests` and that wall's walk really reaches this seam's files, and the parsed `Info.plist` carries both the identifier and the `fetch` mode without having replaced the mesh's wildcard. The frozen identifier is asserted against the LITERAL `MBO.Fernlet.companion-refresh` rather than against `CompanionRefresh.taskIdentifier`, which put the constant on both sides of an `==` and could not fail. The `deliveryAbsorbed` arm is unreachable through the ordinary item-3 path (the handler completes synchronously) and is driven re-entrantly rather than shipped untested. What only a device can prove is named and claimed nowhere. Gated on the `s3-grep` CI step. **2026-09-21 (item 2c):** gains `aRefusedRegistrationSpendsNoEdgeBudget`, driven one past the cap so the cell is about the sixty-fifth audit line, not the count. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/CompanionRefreshPipelineTests.swift` | **P10 item 4**: the refresh handler driven end to end — the step order as a whole trace (a publish that ran before the day roll would publish yesterday under today's key, and per-step booleans cannot tell that story), the reload decision, every failure arm, the outcome→`setTaskCompleted(success:)` table derived from `CaseIterable` so a ninth outcome cannot ship without a stated answer, and the two cancellation boundaries driven from INSIDE a chosen step rather than by racing a yield against one. The last section runs `CompanionRefreshWiring.steps(for:)` — the five production expressions, byte for byte — against a real test store, because a suite of fakes would be equally green if `rollDay` were bound to something that did not roll the day; what is left untested there is `FernletStoreAccess.shared.load()` itself. It also carries the ACQUIRE path's own cells (decision D-10.4.1): a store built without a gateway takes one later, a store that has one refuses a second however often it is asked, and an attach arriving after the workout sync was built is refused rather than leaving the sync on one gateway while the store claims another — all three asserted through `attachHealthKitServiceIfMissing(_:)`'s own `Bool` since item 4's verify retired the test-only `attachedHealthKitService` reader. That verify also added the refusal and day-boundary cells: either adjustment switched on without its bridge ends the run before the roll (D-10.4.6), both off publishes because the identity IS the app's number, an undrained widget row on a NEW day runs the whole tail (D-10.4.8), and a warm midnight roll that republished through the store's own mirror still names that reload in its trace. Installs no `FernletAuditLog` capture anywhere: the registry is process-global and accumulates across parallel suites, and `CompanionRefreshSchedulingTests` already captures the same prefix — everything here is asserted as a VALUE instead, which is why the pipeline returns one. Gated on the `s3-grep` CI step. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/WidgetSnapshotContentEqualityTests.swift` | **P10 item 4**: the content/metadata split "reload timelines only on change" rests on. The trap it exists for: `WidgetSnapshot` is `Equatable` by synthesis and the synthesis includes `computedAt`, stamped `Date()` at every construction — so `==` is FALSE between two snapshots of an unchanged day a millisecond apart, and a handler that diffed with `==` would reload the widget every fifteen minutes forever with a green suite behind it. One cell proves the two notions are different in both directions at once; a flip table covers every content field and a separate cell pins that the table is WHOLE; a `Mirror` field-count pin reds when a field is added to the struct without being classified — the failure a hand-written comparison always has and never announces. The metadata claim is made MECHANICALLY rather than in prose: `computedAt` is counted as code under `App/FernletWidgets` (three sites, all writes, all in the shared-model twin), so a family that starts drawing it reds here instead of leaving the refresh silently skipping reloads a person can see. The mirror's own decision is counted through the `reloadTimelines` closure the type already injects, and a last cell pins that the unconditional `publish(_:)` path still reloads every time. Gated on the `s3-grep` CI step. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/CompanionEmotionEngineTests.swift` | Companion emotions (2026-09-24): the precedence table row by row, every rule alone and in conflict, tone invariants over a 105,840-cell grid, the sleep window, and the widget timeline (transitions, time stability, whole seconds, live agreement, midnight scoping, DST). |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/CompanionEmotionPresentationTests.swift` | The emotion from the app's side: no cheerful face on a gentle/unwell day through the renderer's resolver, the spoken status, the store's inputs and cue switch, and the widget snapshot's emotion timeline (publishable tokens, bedtime moment, round trip, time stability, legacy decode). |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/CompanionEmotionPrivacyTests.swift` | The emotion is never persisted/synced/on the friend wire: not `Codable`, a reviewed file allowlist, the friend fold and `DailyHealthScore` keys unchanged, and no heart on the widget path. |

| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/PhotoDirectoryIsolationTests.swift` | Grep-wall keeping every test `FernletStore` on its own own-photo root. The three own-photo corpora are shared on-disk state that `resetAll`/`deleteAllData`/the duress wipe all clear, and parallel suites share one process — a store on the process-wide root has its photos deleted out from under it by any concurrent wipe. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/PrivacyPolicyParityTests.swift` | Pins the privacy-policy triple in sync (in-app view, `Docs/Privacy-Policy.md`, `Site/privacy/index.html`): same effective date, same load-bearing clauses. Exists because the in-app copy once shipped without the verifiability paragraph the other two had. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/FernletLockScopeTests.swift` | Per-surface unlock scopes: one unlock covers ONE locked surface. Before scoping, unlocking the progress-photo strip also opened the Private tab, with only an `onDisappear` re-lock in between — which a covering sheet or scene transition legitimately suppresses. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/DuressLockTests.swift` | Duress PIN steps 1–4: the duress verifier's own salt, the unconditional derivation that closes the timing oracle, duress-FIRST ordering in every credential entry point, and the keyless decoy session. The property under test is indistinguishability from a benign unlock — same state, same audit line, no attempt/cooldown residue — while never handing over the real content key. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/DuressDecoyAndWipeTests.swift` | Duress steps 5–6: the decoy must change NOTHING on disk (an in-memory flag riding the existing hide machinery, so the real passcode restores every surface exactly), and the silent wipe's crypto-erase must actually destroy. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/DuressRecoveryTests.swift` | Duress steps 7–8: `DuressMode.recoveryLock` destroys every local way into the content key while KEEPING the sealed corpus and a recovery blob only the user's own second device can open, plus the two in-person ceremonies that enroll that device and get the key back. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/DuressHardeningTests.swift` | The properties the duress PIN was supposed to have and did not — each suite pins one confirmed defect from the adversarial review (e.g. a duress session can never hold the `.appLockSettings` scope, the one screen that announces a duress code exists). |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/DuressSetupUITests.swift` | The duress setup screen's testable half: `DuressSetupAvailability` (which response may be armed, whether the recovery device may be un-enrolled) and the rejection copy the view surfaces rather than restates. Unit-level despite the name. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/BackupExclusionLaunchGateTests.swift` | The default-on backup-exclusion launch gate — fresh installs default excluded, existing installs get a one-time honest prompt, nobody is silently flipped — plus the `LocalFernletRepository` day-blob exclusion that closes the last local Fernlet-data file the Privacy & Data toggle did not reach. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/OwnPhotoKeyBindingTests.swift` | The follow-on flip: the own-photos at-rest key becomes device-bound and the dual-open fallback goes away. Runs against REAL keychain rows (isolated `UserDefaults` for the latch/consent) because the thing under test is a keychain attribute. Since the crypto standardization round retired `OwnPhotoKeyMigrator`, the gate's first half reads `MediaAtRestFormatMigrationLatch`, and `aWallKeySealedOwnFileNoLongerBlocksTheGateAndIsLostAcrossTheFlip` pins what that swap COST — a recorded trade, not a surprise. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/SealedPhotoBackupTests.swift` | The own-photo escrow route: one sealed CloudKit record per photo id plus a sealed manifest written LAST as the commit marker, driven over a mock record database with a planted escrow identity. Pins incrementality — sealing photo B leaves A's record byte-identical, which a chunked scheme cannot do. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/SealedBackupPayloadCoverageTests.swift` | Journal narratives and intimacy logs as first-class sealed-backup payloads, driven through `SealedBackupCoordinator` with a fake context: insert-into-empty, the one-way divergence latch, the locked-key deferral, and the empty-store-clobber guard, all against isolated sealed stores. Also the app-lock-reset owner hold over restores AND re-uploads (the escrow adopt included), and the paused period export (review C-U2-R1, C-U2-R2). Since unit B2 the intimacy cases are the v2 merge: a populated store is merged into (`intimacyRestoreMergesIntoAPopulatedStore`), a hidden store is refused at the seam and never reads as empty, the journal's day-skeleton writeback no longer gates the intimacy merge, and an intimacy backup that was off at an app-lock reset uploads through the engine and is re-sealed at the next visit after an escrow adopt. Since unit B3 the journal cases are the v2 engine's too: E1 holds an empty store's export until its restore resolved (then empty is real), entries no key opens pause before any save, the merge runs on a device already in use and into a populated store, every restore and export is held after an app-lock reset until the owner's release (which merges the copy into the post-reset store before the union backs up), a diverged store seeds its marker resolved, and duress refuses the journal merge. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/IntimacyLogRepositoryTests.swift` | The sealed-backup surface on `IntimacyLogRepository`: keyless row count, paged reader in a TOTAL order, the one-way "this device has diverged" latch (now the one-time seed of the intimate-log restore marker), and — unit B2, BV12 — the keyless id snapshot, the classified read by id, the restore MERGE (absent inserted with its stamps and a capped note, local kept with only a missing link filled, dead replaced, never a delete, idempotent, all-or-nothing) and the keyless delete by id. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/CoachPlanExchangeTests.swift` | The manual coach exchange (clipboard export → pasted `CoachPlan` → dated `PlannedWorkout` rows). The paste path takes untrusted text, so most of the suite is what the importer REFUSES: oversize blobs, truncated JSON, plans over the day cap, exercises with no safety metadata. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/MessagesCatalogPublisherTests.swift` | The app's Messages catalog publisher over a library it cannot share whole (2026-09-23): a recipe or plan the exchange rules refuse (a web import with more than 24 servings, a plan name past the card bound) is left out while every shareable item still publishes — one refusal used to throw out of the whole publish and leave the extension on a stale catalog. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/ExchangeMessageEnvelopeV2Tests.swift` | The version-2 Messages envelope: the wire checked against Foundation's own `NSData` deflater/inflater, the tight 3,719-byte frame bound, inflate bombs / truncation / lying lengths / foreign frames, invalid documents, capacity on a realistic 40-step recipe (v1: 5 steps, v2: all 40), and two version-1 cards minted by the untouched 2026-09-23 build plus the version-1 content-hash pre-images as literals — the old wire can never silently stop opening. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/ExchangeMultipartRecipeTests.swift` | A recipe made in parts as an exchange packet and a Messages card: one-part recipes stay packet v1 byte-for-byte, recipes in parts are v2, the v2 content hash checked against an independently built pre-image, each version limited to its own shape, and the salad-with-homemade-dressing fixture fitting a card with room to spare. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/MessagesReceivedItemTests.swift` | `FernletMessagesReceivedItem.resolve` (2026-09-30, the owner's "receiving a recipe pops up blank" report): a card minted by the sender's own encoder opens as that recipe or plan with the packet's own title (multipart parts and the suggested start day kept), the golden version-1 and version-2 cards still open, and a missing, foreign, future-version, truncated, re-encoded, oversize or hash-failing URL is `.invalid` — never an empty screen, never a throw. Gated in CI on the `messages-envelope` line of `s3-wall.yml`. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/LocaleCorrectnessTests.swift` | Locale Phase 0: the month grid must start its week where `Calendar.firstWeekday` says. `veryShortWeekdaySymbols` is always Sunday-indexed, so rendering it unrotated over `firstWeekday - 1` blanks put every day cell under the wrong letter across most of Europe. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/LocaleTolerantNumberTests.swift` | The shared free-text number parser behind every decimal field: iOS shows `.decimalPad` with the locale's separator, so es/fr/de users type `"2,5"` and bare `Double("2,5")` is nil — the value was silently dropped. Pins both spellings, grouped forms, the ambiguous `"1,500"` split, and the charset guard that keeps hex/inf/nan spellings out of clinical samples. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/MicronutrientBoundsTests.swift` | Micronutrients arrive from two untrusted sources (a peer's recipe payload, a web page's JSON-LD) and were carried into the synced snapshot unbounded. Covers the wire half (`sanitizedForImport()` drops an implausible amount to "not measured" rather than fabricating a clamp) and the presentation half, which must be TOTAL because `Int(_: Double)` traps and already-poisoned days are synced. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/WebScrapingKitTests.swift` | `WebScrapingKit`'s HTML/JSON-LD extraction primitives — net-new in the 2026-08-18 security round. The old `scriptContents(from:)` regex retried from every `<script` position, so a page with a few hundred thousand unclosed tags cost ~N²/2 comparisons for zero matches and could stall the main actor into a watchdog kill. Flood cases prove linearity; equivalence cases pin the rewrite's output against the regex it replaced. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/JournalScoringParityTests.swift` | Journaling scores the same whatever kind of day it was (2026-09-23): every `FeelingTag` earns the same journal component, above no entry, never below its retired value; the overall score no longer depends on the tag; the tag survives as the unweighted `mood` reading the period bridge reads (with the legacy-row fallback). Check-ins count half (2026-09-24): a day of one-tap mood check-ins only earns 0.775 (half the credit above no entry), a written entry that day the full 1.0; cells for check-in only / check-in + entry / entry only / none, the unchanged mood reading, the sealed and pre-marker entries that stay full credit, and both store score paths. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/WeightManagementDeficitTests.swift` | Weight Management's deficit: 10% by default and user-adjustable 0–20% in 5% steps (owner, 2026-09-24): the research note's worked examples, each choice's target, the 1,200/1,500 kcal + RMR floor at every choice (the RMR half binding at 20%), "no deficit rather than a surplus" under the floor, a 1,000-profile invariant grid per choice, range/step enforcement, the settings round-trip, no under-18 exception (owner), and the goal card's copy for every choice. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/WeightManagementDeficitSettingTests.swift` | The user-adjustable deficit from the app's side (2026-09-24): the Nutrition targets row (`WeightManagementDeficitControl`) shows only for Weight Management, reads the value in effect, and goes out of effect under a pinned calorie target; the store setter saves durably and enforces the range; the goal card and the targets card read one number (with a source pin on Settings' wiring); Settings search finds the row. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/SickReadinessTests.swift` | Marking today unwell forces readiness to `needs rest` (spec §6a), at once and across the save rebuild; the recommendation is `.light`, never nil; Home's and the launch thought's rest line, the companion prompt's rest-first ordering, the Trends display fork; Move's light-only chips, never-push copy (`MoveRestDayCopy`) and the un-guided rest plan. |

Screenshots attached. Both defects reproduced on iPhone SE (3rd gen), 17e, 17 and 17 Pro Max. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletUITests/TabBarKeyboardUITests.swift` | The floating tab bar stays behind the keyboard (2026-10-01): on a tab root (Private → Worry box), a page pushed one level (the Food tab's Recipe book search) and the editor pushed three levels (Recipe book → Create → Manual entry) the Home tab keeps its keyboard-down frame, lies below the keyboard's top and is not hittable; the pushed editor's Save rests on the keyboard (36pt, not 128.7pt as when the bar's reservation stacked on it); the meal-logged toast, logged with the search field focused, rests above the keyboard and is undone. The three bar tests were red on main on iPhone 17, iOS 26.5 (the bar rode the keyboard everywhere); the toast test is red with the bar pinned but the toast left inside the bar's inset (Undo at y 731.7, behind the keyboard). Frames and screenshots attached. |

Food → Recipe book search, the pushed manual editor's name and Qty pad, the recipe and recipe-book sheets, Settings → Goal & nutrition's calorie pad, First aid's worry editor, and the stack-less Sleep sheet as the control: one button labelled "Done" just above the keys, and tapping it puts the keyboard away. A second pass (2026-10-01) covers hosts no sheet chrome reaches, which had no Done at all: the day edit sheet's sleep-hours decimal pad and the pre-log meal review's Qty pad (sheets presented bare by a page), onboarding's age number pad (the window's root view), and the Private tab's Worry box composer (a tab page with no stack). |

## Documentation

DocC landing pages (one per SPM module, plus the app and extension targets) are the orientation
layer and live in-source at `Documentation.docc/`; the tables below are the planning-doc layer.

### Orientation

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `Docs/FernletSpecificationV3.md` | Canonical product & architecture specification, reconciled with the current implementation on 2026-09-14. Explicit current-status blocks override retained historical prototype notes. |
| `Docs/ImplementationPlan.md` | Historical phase definitions and rationale, plus the reconciled 2026-09-14 status table and current release/mesh ordering. |
| `Docs/FileIndex.md` | This file index. |
| `Docs/ProximityFunctionIndex.md` | Function-level map for proximity, mesh, transport, identity, trust, audit, friend-photo, recipe-share, presence, activities, clothing shop, messaging, and moderation code. Read before adding proximity behavior. |
| `Docs/StoreRepositoryFunctionIndex.md` | Function-level map for `FernletStore`, repositories, persistence, storage preferences, snapshot saves, and the extracted store services. Read before adding data-mutation or save/load behavior. |

### Privacy commitments, walls, and process

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `Docs/Privacy-Policy.md` | The published privacy policy text, mirrored by the in-app view and the hosted page; the triple is kept in sync by `PrivacyPolicyParityTests`. |
| `Docs/Verifiability.md` | Standing commitment: the bridge between the promises in the privacy policy and the machinery that backs them. Every guarantee names the exact command, test, or file an outside auditor can run, so "trust us" can be replaced with "run this". The repo is public and `fernlet.com` is deployed. |
| `Docs/Release-Process.md` | Standing process, and the governance half of `Verifiability.md`: branch protection, code-owner review, signed release tags, checksum publication, and the two one-time publish steps. The walls make a privacy regression loud; this makes shipping one attributable and gated. |
| `Docs/No-Tracking-Wall.md` | The enforced no-tracking boundary: permitted network destinations and why each exists, mechanical vs. policy enforcement, and how to add a destination. Backed by `NoTrackingBoundaryTests`. |
| `Docs/PrivacyWipeCoverage.md` | The enforced delete-all coverage checklist, backed by `PrivacyWipeCoverageTests`. |
| `Docs/Crypto-Domain-Separation.md` | The cryptographic domain registry: the inventory of its 81 entries across five families (the tables list 69 and name the twelve they lack), the rule that purpose spellings are FROZEN protocol data (never localized, never assembled from input, never casually renamed), transcript framing and the `91c3956` mismatch it records, which formats are version-marked and the fact that NOTHING reads an older one any more, the procedure and review checklist for adding a domain, and — at length — what the two boundary suites can and cannot catch, including the 6 remaining `// cryptographic-domain:` escape-hatch sites — all annotations, no reader left — and how the count reached them. Backed by `CryptographicPurposeBoundaryTests` (a purpose is USED) and `CryptographicDomainSeparationTests` (the registry's values are USABLE for separation). §8 (2026-10-02) covers ProximityKit's namespace: labels the host supplies as source literals, roles ProximityKit fixes, `.fernlet` in FernletConnections, the 38 registry twins `ProximityNamespaceGoldenTests` holds equal until they retire, and the epoch label `fernlet.mesh.epoch.v1`, now inside the joint prefix check. |
| `Docs/Power-of-10-Swift.md` | The Power-of-10 wall: NASA's ten rules adapted to Swift/SwiftUI, rule by rule — what is enforced by `Scripts/power-of-10-scan.py` + `PowerOfTenBoundaryTests` (zero baseline + reasoned allowlist) and what is enforced by review. Read before writing any function. |
| `Docs/SPM-Module-Carveup-Plan.md` | The `FernletKit` carve-up: the S3 compile-time privacy wall and cross-platform layering. One optional item remains (§14 AI-file inversions). |
| `Docs/App-Privacy-Nutrition-Labels.md` | Draft answers for the App Store Connect App Privacy questionnaire (owner-entered). |
| `Docs/Export-Compliance-Encryption.md` | Encryption export compliance: mass-market, ECCN 5D992.c, self-classified under License Exception ENC with no CCATS. Materially revised 2026-08-19 — the determination held, its *filing consequences* did not: §740.17(e)(3) was rewritten in 2021 to cover only encryption components and "executable software", so the annual February 1 report is almost certainly not owed, and the earlier text of this document asserted a recurring legal obligation that does not exist. |
| `Docs/Export-Self-Classification-Memo.md` | The self-classification memorandum itself — the record §740.17(b)(1) obliges the developer to have made and be able to produce. Five-year retention past the last export. Engineering and regulatory analysis, explicitly not legal advice; §8 lists the two questions that warrant counsel. |

The original review is preserved as written; where it and the *Disposition* section disagree, the Disposition is correct. |

### Feature and design plans

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `Docs/Calorie-Deficit-Research-2026-09-23.md` | Evidence for a gentler Weight Management starting deficit (CDC, NHLBI, AHA/ACC/TOS, NICE NG246, DGA, NIDDK/Hall, small-changes literature, CALERIE 2, safety floors, AAP/weight-inclusive sources), worked examples, six options, and the shipped recommendation (10% + 1,200/1,500 kcal/RMR floor). Owner decisions 2026-09-24: 10% is the baseline and users can change it (0–20% in 5% steps, floors always); no under-18 exception; the spec's "no deficit" wording is still owner-held. |

| `Docs/Plan-ProximityKit-Network-Migration-2026-08-27.md` | The authority for the ProximityKit network migration (QUIC transport, durable sessions, partition + merge, the routed store, feature routing, the app-layer run policy, background continuation): one section per phase, each marked BUILT with landing SHAs and its §n.1–§n.4 close-out (what landed / deviations / findings not fixed / acceptance evidence), and one handoff section per boundary (§19–§25). **§13 (P7) is IMPLEMENTED, UNBUILT** — the one phase whose heading is not BUILT, because nothing in it has compiled. |
| `Docs/Mesh-P9-Item4-Design-2026-09-20.md` | P9 item 4's survey of the MultipeerConnectivity retirement, written before any code landed: why the launcher's row was wrong about HEAD (the friend mesh still ships on MC; the QUIC radio is members-only, so a first meeting between two phones that have never met cannot found a mesh on it), the D-4.1/D-4.2/D-4.3 decision with D-4.4, the acceptance criterion, the walls that bite, and seven appendices — the rule-7 cell verbatim and six anchored patches (`Info.plist`, `MeshNetworkManager.swift`, `MeshTransportSelection.swift`, `TransportNeutralityBoundaryTests.swift`, docs, tests), every hunk marked `[SPLIT: NOW]` (landed as 9.4-NOW) or `[SPLIT: LATER]` (the cutover). *Row added 2026-09-21; the file had shipped without one.* |
| `Docs/Mesh-Stranger-Admission-Design-2026-09-21.md` | The design D-4.3 was waiting for: how the QUIC radio admits a stranger at a first meeting so the MC transport can be retired. Compares the two radios' admission stages side by side, names two owed items the survey did not (the meshID check precedes the roster check, so a mesh-less joiner dialing an open mesh needs a target-mesh rule; the `browsed peers=` log level), and prices three options — provisional admission while the join doors are open (recommended: MC's own posture, key proven, no screen, no wire field, no persisted surface; changes only the survey's `MeshNetworkManager.swift` edits 5 and 6), a two-scan QR pre-admission (strongest, needs a pre-session screen), and keeping MC for admission (ruled out) — with trust consequence, UI cost, tests owed and appendix patches changed for each. Ends with the one-sentence D-4.3 decision, D-4.4 riding with it as the legacy sweep. |
| `Docs/Mesh-Migration-Loop-Ledger-P9.md`, `…-Cutover-2026-09-21.md`, `…-Device-2026-09-22.md`, `…-Owner-Calls-2026-09-22.md` | The loop ledgers still in the tree. P9's and the cutover round's are read from disk by `MeshP9AcceptanceTests` — the record of item 0's owner block, 9.4-LATER, P9-3-A and the owner's D-4.3/D-4.4 answers — so they stay. The device round's and the owner-calls round's (with its launcher, `Docs/Next-Round-Prompt-Owner-Calls-2026-09-22.md`) carry the owed soak read-out and leave once it is read. Every other round's launcher, ledger and progress log is local session material — out of the tree since 2026-09-22; `e6a3b48` holds the last copies. |
